Only there do functable, funcdomain, functodomain and funcfromdomain show up, at the top of the list (type funct…, funcd…, functo…, funcf…; also toDomain / fromDomain / TableName). They are generated from the struct fields and only for those that do not have the function yet.

  • Domain by name: NoteModel → note.Note (package note, type Note). Fields are passed in order to the constructor and read with getters (n.Title()).
  • Real constructor: if the file imports …/note and the package is in the module, it looks there for Reconstitute, Rehydrate, Restore, Hydrate, FromPersistence or Load. If none is found, Reconstitute stays as an editable placeholder (Tab).
  • Several models in the file: functodomain NoteModel, and fromDomain becomes fromNoteDomain to avoid a clash.
  • The domain package import is added by goimports on save. Formatting (alignment of ID: n.ID(),) is fixed by gofumpt.

Contextual completion (utils/gosmart.lua)

Reads the treesitter tree to know where you are and offers only what applies. The documentation window shows the code that will be inserted.

When starting a declaration (column 0)

Type func, type, New… or the name of a struct. There, loose buffer words and the generic LSP func/type do not show up.

func — function variants:

OptionGenerates
funcsimple function
func errorreturns error (with return nil)
func valuereturns a T
func pointerreturns a *T
func boolreturns bool
func result errorreturns (T, error)
func pointer errorreturns (*T, error)
func ctx errortakes ctx context.Context, returns error
func ctx result errortakes ctx, returns (T, error)
func main / func initthe special functions

Methods per struct (for each struct in the file, e.g. Envelope):

OptionGenerates
func (e *Envelope)method with pointer receiver
func (e *Envelope) errormethod that returns error
func (e Envelope)method with value receiver
func (e Envelope) Stringfmt.Stringer
functablegorm: func (NoteModel) TableName() string with the derived name (NoteModel → notes, Category → categories). Shows up first: just type funct… (or func / TableName). With several structs: functable NoteModel, functable Category… Only in *_model.go or model.go files, and not for structs that already have it.
funcdomaintoDomain + fromDomain at once (see below). Shows up with func or by typing funcd…
functodomainfunc (m NoteModel) toDomain() *note.Note { return note.Reconstitute(m.ID, m.Title, …) }
funcfromdomainfunc fromDomain(n *note.Note) NoteModel { return NoteModel{ID: n.ID(), …} }

Constructor — NewEnvelope shows up for every struct without a NewX and already includes the fields:

func NewEnvelope(kind Kind, id string, sentAt time.Time) *Envelope {
	return &Envelope{
		Kind:   kind,
		ID:     id,
		SentAt: sentAt,
	}
}

Parameters are in lowerCamel (UserID → userID, ID → id). Embedded fields are not included.

funcnew — constructor that returns (*X, error). It is optional: it does not show up in the list until you type funcn… (at package level). Tab/Enter insert it:

func New(id, title, content string) (*Note, error) {
	|   // ← cursor here: validations (max lengths, empties…)
	now := time.Now().UTC()
	return &Note{
		id:        id,
		title:     title,
		content:   content,
		createdAt: now,
		updatedAt: now,
	}, nil
}

time.Time fields whose name starts with created, updated or modified are not parameters: they come from a single now := time.Now().UTC(). Any other time.Time (dueDate) or *time.Time is still a parameter. Without fields of that type, now is not generated.

Contiguous parameters of the same type are grouped. With a single struct in the file it is called New; with several, NewX (and several entries show up: funcnew Note, funcnew Other…).

Reconstitute — the persistence-layer constructor: it takes every field and sets it, with no validation and no now, because the data was valid when it was saved. Optional like funcnew: it shows up when you type recon… (or funcrecon…), 5 letters at least, at package level, once per struct declared in the current file (also inside type ( … ) blocks) that has no such function yet. Structs of other files of the package and generic structs are not offered:

// Reconstitute rehydrates a Note from persistence without validating it.
func Reconstitute(id, title, content string, createdAt, updatedAt time.Time) *Note {
	return &Note{
		id:        id,
		title:     title,
		content:   content,
		createdAt: createdAt,
		updatedAt: updatedAt,
	}
}

Same rules as funcnew for the rest: grouped parameters, lowerCamel names, embedded fields skipped, Reconstitute with one struct in the file and ReconstituteX with several. It is the name toDomain (functodomain, *_model.go) looks for in the domain package.

Getters — the accessors of an aggregate's private fields: no Get prefix, no setters (changes go through methods that mean something). For every unexported field of a struct declared in the current file that has no accessor yet:

func (n *Note) ID() string           { return n.id }
func (n *Note) Title() string        { return n.title }
func (n *Note) CreatedAt() time.Time { return n.createdAt }
func (n *Note) Tags() []string       { return slices.Clone(n.tags) }

Two ways in: at package level type getters (or funcgetters, accessors; 5 letters at least) for all the missing ones of a struct at once, or write func (n *Note) and the menu (it opens by itself) lists one item per missing getter plus all getters. Details: the receiver is the one your other methods use (else the first letter of the struct, pointer); names follow Go's initialisms (id → ID, userId → UserID, url → URL); exported and embedded fields are skipped; a getter you already wrote is not repeated; the bodies come aligned like gofmt writes them. A slice or map is returned as a copy (slices.Clone / maps.Clone, the import comes with goimports on save), so the caller cannot change the aggregate's state through it. settings.go.getters.clone = false returns the field as it is. A pointer field is returned as it is.

package at the top of a file — gopls always offers package main next to package <folder>, and it used to rank first. Now the candidates are judged by where the file is (utils/gopackage.lua):

  • the other .go files of the folder decide: they say package notes → notes first and no main; they say main → main first (_test files count as their package; files with //go:build ignore and comments are not read as neighbours);
  • no neighbours: main leads only under cmd/…, in a file called main.go, or in the module root; anywhere else it is not offered (typing package m… still brings it, below the rest);
  • X_test stays below X, except in a _test.go file, where both are real options.

Repository port — the persistence interface of a domain object, which is almost always the same. Only in the domain layer (a domain/, entities/, model/… folder, the names of settings.agent.ddd.layers.domain, read from the path under the module root) type repository (repo is enough) at package level and each aggregate of the package that has no port yet is offered:

// Repository is the persistence port of the Note aggregate; infrastructure implements it.
type Repository interface {
	Save(ctx context.Context, note *Note) error
	FindByID(ctx context.Context, id string) (*Note, error)
	Delete(ctx context.Context, id string) error
	List(ctx context.Context) ([]*Note, error)
}
  • An aggregate is a struct with an identity: a field id, ID or <Struct>ID; its type is the one in the signatures (string, uuid.UUID, int64, NoteID). Value objects (no id) and *Model, *DTO, *Request, *Response, *Row, *Entity structs get nothing. A struct that already has its port is not offered again.
  • In a package named after the struct (package note) the port is Repository (note.Repository); in any other, NoteRepository.
  • Nothing shows up outside the domain layer, nor in *_model.go files: the port belongs to the domain.
  • settings.go.repository.methods sets the methods and their order (Save, FindByID, Delete, List, Exists).
  • The adapter is the other half: in infrastructure, func (r *noteRepo) offers the methods it is missing (goimpl) and var _ note.Repository = (*noteRepo)(nil) comes from goport.

Strict prefix matching (not fuzzy): only what starts with what you typed shows up.

You typeShows up
f, fu, func, funconly the func variants, the methods and the pending NewX
t, ty, type, typeonly the type variants
New, NewE, Envonly the NewEnvelope constructor
func Neonly NewEnvelope
pac, package, i, v, c…none of this (the rest comes from the LSP and friendly-snippets)
type Foo, func Foonothing: you are naming something

Missing methods to satisfy an interface (utils/goimpl.lua)

If a struct shows signs of implementing an interface and is missing methods, the menu offers exactly those, with the interface's signature and a useful body (0 tokens, no LLM). Two places:

  • After typing the receiver: func (s *noteService) opens the menu by itself (without typing anything) with …all missing (6) and each missing method (Create, Get, ...). Filter as you type (Se → SetDone). The receiver is used as you wrote it (s).
  • When starting the declaration (func at column 0): the same for each struct in the file, with the func (s *noteService) header already in place.

How it knows which interface it implements (the first that fits): var _ NoteService = (*noteService)(nil); a constructor that returns it (func NewNoteService(...) NoteService { return &noteService{...} }); the name (noteService ~ NoteService, postgresNoteRepository ~ domain.NoteRepository, also if the interface is in another package of the module); or what it already implemented. What is already written is not offered again.

Body: if the struct holds a dependency that is an interface (repo domain.NoteRepository) and it has a method with the same shape (parameter and return types; the name does not matter), the body calls it: Get → return s.repo.FindByID(ctx, id), List → s.repo.FindAll(ctx, filter), Delete → s.repo.Delete(ctx, id). If there is no equivalent (Create, Update...) the cursor is left in the body with a return nil, nil (zero values) that compiles. With …all missing each empty body is a tabstop (Tab jumps).

Interfaces from another package of the module are read from disk (the go.mod module) and their types are qualified (Note → domain.Note); missing imports are added by goimports on save. Those from the standard library or from dependencies are not read: for those, gopls already offers <leader>ca → "Declare missing methods of …" on the constructor error (fills everything with panic("unimplemented")).

More helpers without an LLM (0 tokens, they read your code and your go.mod)

They show up in the same func / New… menu, above the rest. They share an index (utils/goindex.lua: interfaces, structs, methods, functions and constants of each file, package and the whole module, cached by date).

  • if err != nil that knows what to return (utils/goiferr.lua): after n, err := s.repo.FindByID(ctx, id) and Enter, the menu opens by itself with the block already built and Enter accepts it (turn off with vim.g.goiferr_preselect = false): the zero values come from the RETURNS of the function containing the cursor (return nil, fmt.Errorf("find by id: %w", err)), and the message from the name of what you called (editable; FindByID → "find by id"). Second variant: return nil, err. In main → log.Fatalf. Inside a func literal it uses ITS returns. In a function that does not return an error it offers nothing. fmt is added by goimports on save.
  • Mappers between layers (utils/gomap.lua): for each struct in the file ending in Response/Request/DTO/Model/Entity/ Record/Input/Output... it looks for its counterpart in the module (NoteResponse → Note; CreateNoteRequest → Note) and on func offers func toNoteResponse(n *domain.Note) NoteResponse, toNoteResponses (list) and func (r NoteResponse) toNote() *domain.Note. Fields by name (ignoring case and _); if the source has the field private and a getter (ID()), it uses the getter; types defined over a basic type are converted (string(n.Status) / domain.Status(r.Status)); whatever does not fit or has no source comes out as // TODO (nothing made up). *_model.go files keep using toDomain/fromDomain from gomodel.
  • Wiring constructors (utils/gowire.lua): in a function (typically main) type New… or the variable name (noteSer): it offers noteService := application.NewNoteService(noteRepo) with the arguments already filled in, for ALL packages of the module (even if you have not imported them yet; goimports adds it). Each argument: a variable of the same type → a variable whose concrete type implements the interface the parameter asks for → a variable named like the parameter → context.Background() → a hole. Variable types come from parameters, var x T, &T{} and the returns of constructors already called.
  • Enum helpers (utils/goenum.lua): with type Status string + its const (...), func offers IsValid, ParseStatus, UnmarshalText and, on numeric enums (iota), String and MarshalText; plus "…enum helpers" with all of them. Only what is missing.
  • Interface from a struct and var _ (utils/goport.lua): inside type NoteService interface { } it offers the exported methods of noteService it does not declare yet (with their signature); and var at column 0 offers var _ NoteService = (*noteService)(nil) for the structs whose interface is recognized (that line is what makes the compiler complain and what goimpl recognizes with certainty).
  • HTTP handlers from a service (utils/gohttp.lua): in a struct NoteHandler { svc application.NoteService }, func (h *NoteHandler) (or func) offers one handler for each service method that is missing, Routes and "all". Framework by go.mod: net/http (Go ≥ 1.22, PathValue), chi, gin or echo (fiber: nothing). Verb by name (Get/List → GET, Create → POST, Update → PUT, Set*/Patch* → PATCH, Delete → DELETE); id → route parameter; a struct in a GET (filter ListFilter) → query string field by field (strconv.Atoi/ParseBool); the rest → JSON body (anonymous struct with snake_case tags); (T, error) → 200 with the JSON (201 on POST), only error → 204. For net/http and chi, a separate item with writeJSON/writeError if they do not exist.

After type Envelope

Offers struct, interface and func(…). If you already typed struct, nothing shows up.

Inside interface { }

Method signatures: method, method error, method value, method result error, method ctx error, method ctx result error, embed interface.

Struct name in value position → literal already filled

Type the name (x := Env) and Enter inserts the full struct, with no extra steps:

o := Envelope{
	Kind: ,
	ID: "",
	SentAt: time.Time{},
}

Tab jumps from value to value (each value comes with its zero default selected). It applies when what you type is a value:

ContextExample
right side of := / =o := Env, o = &Env
returnreturn Env
field valueOuter{In: Env
call argumentprocess(Env, process(a, Env

In type position it does not trigger and normal gopls completion continues: var e Env, parameters func(a Env, new(Env, []Env. Only for structs of the same package; the bare gopls/buffer name is hidden to avoid duplicating the item.

Inside a literal Envelope{ }

For structs of the same package (same file or another .go in the same folder with the same package). The menu opens by itself, without typing anything, at three moments: when typing { (leaves Envelope{|}), when accepting Envelope{} from the type menu, and when pressing Enter after { or after a field: value,:

OptionDoes
fill all fieldsinserts all missing fields, with default values; Tab jumps from value to value
Kind, ID, SentAt…a single field: Kind: █, with the cursor on the value
  • Fields you already put do not show up again; it repeats after each field: value,.
  • Default values by type: string → "", numbers → 0, bool → false, pointers/slices/maps → nil, time.Time{}, Inner{} (struct from the file).
  • Works with Envelope{, &Envelope{ and elements of []Envelope{ {…} }.
  • With Envelope{|} on a single line, it opens the lines for you: leaves Envelope{ / fields / }.
  • A package x_test does not see the structs of package x (it does not compile without qualifying), so it does not offer them.
  • It does not trigger in positional literals (T{a, b}) nor inside another field's value (e.g. call arguments).
  • Structs from other packages (http.Client{): gopls completes it as usual. For those, use <leader>gs (GoFillStruct).
  • Buffer words do not show up here, and gopls fields are hidden to avoid duplicates.

Inside a function

Statement snippets show up (below). Declaration ones are hidden. Inside a struct none show up.

Declaring sentinel errors (goerrors)

Type only the error name: the rest appears by itself, in gray, and grows with each letter (utils/goerrors.lua).

ErrDescriptionTooLong|  →  ErrDescriptionTooLong = errors.New("description too long")
WhereYou typeWritten
inside var ( … )ErrNotFoundErrNotFound = errors.New("not found")
after varErrInvalidIDErrInvalidID = errors.New("invalid id")
package levelErrEmptyTitlevar ErrEmptyTitle = errors.New("empty title")
KeyAction
Tabaccepts the gray text (cursor at end of line)
Enteraccepts it and opens a new line (ideal to continue with the next error in the block)
Escdiscards it
  • Splits the name by capitals and lowercases it (TitleTooLong → title too long, HTTPError → http error).
  • Also accepts unexported names (errNotFound). It needs Err + capital letter (Error… does not trigger).
  • It only shows up with the cursor at the end of the line; while there is a suggestion the completion menu does not open.
  • The errors import is added by goimports on save.
  • It does not show up inside functions (sentinels are not declared there).

Statement snippets (nvim/snippets/go.json)

Full names: you find them by typing any part (wrap → errorsis…, ctx → the context ones).

SnippetInserts
iferrif err != nil { return err } (err is left selected so you can change it; only inside functions)
errorsisif errors.Is(err, ErrX) { … }
errorsasvar t *MyError; if errors.As(err, &t) { … }
errorsentinelvar ErrX = errors.New("…") (package level only; message derived from the name: see above)
contexttimeoutctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(…) + defer cancel()
contextcancelcontext.WithCancel + defer cancel()
waitgroupsync.WaitGroup over a range
errgrouperrgroup.WithContext + g.Go + g.Wait
mutexmu.Lock() + defer mu.Unlock()
onceonce.Do(func() { … })
selectcontextselect with ctx.Done()
forrangeindexfor i, v := range items
forrangeintfor i := range n (Go 1.22+)
testsubtestt.Run("name", func(t *testing.T) { … })
testtabletable-driven test with subtests (package level only)
testhelperhelper with t.Helper() (package level only)
httphandlerjsonHTTP handler that responds with JSON (package level only)
jsondecodebodydecode the body's JSON
slogslog.Info/Error/Warn/Debug(…)
deferfuncdefer func() { … }()
embed//go:embed (package level only)

The friendly-snippets ones that are not duplicated are also still available (if, ife, ir, for, forr, switch, sel, go, df, fp, ff, lp, make, tf, tdt, hand…). The friendly declaration ones (func, meth, tys, tyi, tyf, finit, fmain) are hidden at package level because gosmart replaces them with more variants.

Where everything lives

FileRole
lua/languages/go.luadeclaration: gopls, goimports, gofumpt, golangci-lint, govet
lua/languages/actions/go.lua<leader>g menu actions
lua/gosetup/<leader>gS: presets (presets/<topic>/<variant>), vars.lua (module, Go version, main), init.lua (topics, flows)
lua/plugins/languages/go.luago.nvim (tag_transform = "snakecase", tag_options = false)
lua/servers/gopls.luagopls settings
lua/utils/gotags.luablink source: struct tags
lua/utils/gosmart.luablink source: func/type/methods/constructor/interface + filters
lua/utils/goindex.luashared index (treesitter over text, per file/package/module) for the helpers below
lua/utils/goiferr.lua · gomap.lua · gowire.lua · goenum.lua · goport.lua · gohttp.luaif err with returns, mappers, wiring, enums, interface from a struct, HTTP handlers
lua/utils/goimpl.luamissing methods to satisfy an interface (receiver and declaration), bodies that call the dependency
lua/utils/gomodel.lua*_model.go-only shortcuts: functable, funcdomain, functodomain, funcfromdomain
lua/utils/goerrors.luaghost text for sentinel errors (ErrX → ErrX = errors.New("x")); Tab/Enter are wired in blink.lua
lua/plugins/editing/blink.luaregisters the sources and filters (buffer, lsp, snippets)
snippets/go.jsonstatement snippets

Notes

  • If the treesitter tree comes out broken (unclosed brace), the filter prefers to show everything rather than hide something useful.
  • To tweak or add func/type/method variants: FUNCS, TYPES and METHODS tables in utils/gosmart.lua.
  • To add a statement snippet: snippets/go.json, and if it only applies inside functions, add its name to FUNC_ONLY in gosmart.lua.

Languages · docs/cheatsheets/dart-flutter.md

Dart / Flutter

Filetype: dart. The keys are in CHEATSHEET.md (generated; not duplicated here).

Tools

WhatWith
LSPdartls, managed by flutter-tools.nvim (not by vim.lsp.enable); not started in light mode
Format (on save and <leader>fm)dart_format
Treesitterdart
Debuggernvim-dap + nvim-dap-view (plugins/tools/dap.lua), loaded only when you debug
Code generationdart run build_runner from lua/dartgen (watch in the background, state in the statusline)
Completionutils/dartsmart.lua (declarations for the project's stack) + the LSP + the Flutter snippets of friendly-snippets

Run, hot reload, debug

Four keys start an app (lua/dartrun):

KeyDoes
<leader>drrun on the device and entry point you used last, at once
<leader>ddthe same, with the debug panel open from the start
<leader>dsthe same in profile mode (performance: no debugger, <leader>dt opens DevTools)
<leader>dDasks the device, the entry point (lib/main_dev.dart…) and the mode (debug / profile / release)

The device, the entry point and the last choice are remembered per project (stdpath("state")/dartrun.json), so after the first run r, d and s ask nothing; with a device missing they ask only for it (a single connected device is taken without asking). Devices come from flutter devices --machine (~0.5 s) and are sorted last used, phones, emulators, desktop, web; the ones the project has no folder for (web/, ios/…) are not offered. <leader>ds and <leader>dD while an app runs stop it and start the new one (r/d never interrupt it). The iOS simulator only runs debug (Flutter's own rule; Android emulators do run profile). Project configurations you declare yourself (require("flutter-tools").setup_project, with flavors or dart-define) win for r and d; a plain Dart package (no Flutter) is run by flutter-tools as before. With FVM, the project's SDK (.fvm/flutter_sdk) is used when it is installed.

Statusline. A chip follows the app: ◌ Pixel 7 while it builds, ▶ Pixel 7 running, ▶ Pixel 7 · profile (blue: it is not the build you debug), ⏸ Pixel 7 stopped at a breakpoint (yellow). It disappears when the session ends.

Quiet by default. A run puts nothing on the screen: the app's log goes to a hidden buffer (<leader>dl opens or closes it; settings.flutter.log = "split" opens it in a bottom split at every run, as before), the debug panel (nvim-dap-view) opens with <leader>dd or when the app stops at a breakpoint, and its errors still arrive as notes. <leader>dH hides whatever is open (log, debug panel, widget outline); with nothing open it shows the log.

Profile in practice. <leader>ds on a phone (or Linux/Chrome): hot reload keeps working, breakpoints do not. Open DevTools with <leader>dt for the Performance and CPU tabs. Profile on a simulator or an emulator measures the host's speed, not the device's: use a real device for numbers.

It runs through nvim-dap, so a breakpoint (<leader>db, <leader>dB with a condition) stops it in any run; <leader>dd does the same explicitly and opens the panel at once. Saving a .dart file hot-reloads the running app and saving pubspec.yaml runs pub get (flutter-tools). While stopped: <leader>dc continue, <leader>dn step over, <leader>di into, <leader>dO out; the panel (watches, scopes, breakpoints, threads, REPL) opens and closes with the session (<leader>du toggles it). The launch configuration is flutter-tools' own ("Launch flutter" on lib/main.dart, or the target of its project config): never empty dap.configurations.dart, or nothing launches.

Code generation (build_runner)

For packages with build_runner in pubspec.yaml (freezed, json_serializable, riverpod_generator, drift…):

  • <leader>dg starts / stops build_runner watch for the file's package, in the background. The statusline shows gen … while it builds, gen when it is up to date and gen ✗ N when it failed.
  • A failed build puts its errors as diagnostics on the file and line they point at (source = the builder, e.g. json_serializable) plus one note; the next good build clears them and says Codegen fixed.
  • <leader>dL shows the whole output; <leader>dG builds once.
  • Opening a file whose generated part (part 'x.g.dart';, part 'x.freezed.dart';) does not exist says so once.
  • Light mode: <leader>dg builds once instead of keeping a watch (a Dart VM stays resident).

Both output formats are understood (build_runner ≥ 2.5: Built with build_runner… / E builder on file:; older: [INFO] Succeeded after… / [SEVERE]). build_runner 2.16 removed --delete-conflicting-outputs: it is not passed.

Tests inside the editor

KeyRuns
<leader>dmthe test or group under the cursor (the whole file when the cursor is outside any)
<leader>dMthe file
<leader>dAevery test of the package
<leader>d.the last run again
<leader>dTjumps between lib/x.dart and test/x_test.dart, creating the test file (not saved) with its imports and a group
<leader>dwthe raw output of the last run (a compile error that stopped it before any test, toolchain warnings)

flutter test or dart test (whichever the package is) with the JSON reporter (lua/darttest). Each test line gets a mark as it finishes (✓ with its time, ✗, ○ skipped, … running) and the statusline counts test ✓12 ✗1 …. A failure becomes a diagnostic on the line its stack trace points at (the expect that failed) with the expected/actual text and whatever the test printed; the failures go to the quickfix list and one note sums the run up (Tests failed: 2 of 3 · 1 skipped · calc add fails). Results are kept per file, so a test file opened later shows them. A test is found by its literal name, groups included (--plain-name "calc add sums"): a name with interpolation ('renders $x') cannot be targeted, run its group or the file. Flutter compiles before the first test: a single widget test takes ~45 s the first time on a big app, and much less afterwards.

Dependencies

<leader>dU writes, next to each dependency of pubspec.yaml that is behind, what it can move to: ↑ 2.5.6 (green: pub upgrade takes it, the constraint allows it), latest 3.0.0 (change the constraint) (yellow) or discontinued / security advisory (red); saving pubspec.yaml clears them. <leader>dP adds packages (name, dev:name, name:^1.2.0, several at once) with pub add; its error, if any, is one note. Both ask pub.dev (network). <leader>dp is pub get (saving pubspec.yaml also runs it).

Translations

Saving an .arb file in a package with l10n.yaml runs flutter gen-l10n in the background, so AppLocalizations.of(context).newKey exists for the LSP at once; a mistake (an ICU syntax error, a missing placeholder) comes back as one note with gen-l10n's own message.

Completion for the stack

At the start of a top-level declaration (column 0) the completion offers what pubspec.yaml says the package uses, named after the file (snippet_filter_bar.dart → SnippetFilterBar, cart_provider.dart → cart):

ItemNeedsGives
consumer, consumerstatefulflutter_riverpod / hooks_riverpodConsumerWidget, ConsumerStatefulWidget + its State
riverpod, riverpodnotifierriverpod_annotation@riverpod function provider, @riverpod Notifier class
freezed, freezedunionfreezed@freezed abstract class (with fromJson when json_serializable is there), sealed union
stateless, statefulflutterStatelessWidget, StatefulWidget + its State

Accepting one also adds the import and part lines it needs and the file lacks (utils/dartheader.lua), so part 'x.g.dart'; is never forgotten. They are applied after the snippet (the source's execute), not as additionalTextEdits: blink applies those after expanding the snippet, and in a new file an insert at the typed line lands below the class.

The agent with Dart

  • <leader>at (convert) follows the stack: JSON → freezed classes (or @JsonSerializable() without freezed), a SQL table → a drift Table. The import and part lines are added when you accept, and a snake_case key the model left without @JsonKey(name: …) gets it by rule (it would deserialize as null). Skipped when build.yaml has field_rename.
  • After accepting a change, saving runs dart analyze on that file (machine format) and blames only the errors inside what was accepted.
  • "Fix from output" (<leader>aAo / aAO) reads flutter analyze and dart analyze output.
  • Generated files (*.g.dart, *.freezed.dart, *.gr.dart, *.mocks.dart, protobuf) are never edited by the agent (explain/ask still work there) and are left out of its context and of code search (utils/generated.lua).

Setup (<leader>dS)

analysis_options.yaml (strict), .editorconfig, .fvmrc (Flutter version pin), GitHub Actions (flutter pub get, analyze, test), a Dart server Dockerfile and its .dockerignore. Presets live in lua/langsetup/presets/dart/ and are filled from pubspec.yaml (name, sdk). Never overwrites: what is there is opened.

Pitfalls

  • Requires pubspec.yaml: it detects whether the project is Flutter (flutter: block) or plain Dart and uses the right command (flutter test / dart test, etc.).
  • The Dart debug adapter pauses "on entry" and resumes by itself: calling continue there gets nvim-dap's "not focused on any stopped thread" question.
  • Neovim's snippet parser rejects nested placeholders (${1:Future<${2:T}>}): keep one placeholder per type.

Languages · docs/cheatsheets/typescript.md

TypeScript / JS

Filetypes: typescript, typescriptreact, javascript, javascriptreact. Pack typescript.

Tools

WhatWith
LSPvtsls (TS/JS), eslint (where the project has an eslint config), biome (where it has a biome.json)
Format on savebiome where the project has a biome.json, prettier everywhere else
Linteslint_d
Extratreesitter (javascript, typescript, tsx, jsdoc), nvim-ts-autotag, package-info (versions in package.json), Tailwind color preview

The package manager comes from the lockfile: pnpm-lock.yaml → pnpm, yarn.lock → yarn, bun.lock(b) → bun, otherwise npm. The project's own binaries run with its exec (pnpm exec, yarn, bunx, npx --no-install): nothing is ever downloaded.

<leader>t menu (only in ts/tsx/js/jsx)

The keys are in CHEATSHEET.md. What they do:

  • Scripts: a picker of the package.json scripts, dev, build, lint (eslint when there is no script).
  • Typecheck: tsc --noEmit on the whole project, its errors in the quickfix list (no terminal to read).
  • Tests in the editor: the runner comes from package.json (vitest or jest in the dependencies or the test script, else bun test in a bun project). The test under the cursor is the it/test (or describe) around it, .only/.skip/.each included, selected by its full name; the file; the project; the last run again. Marks per test line, failures as diagnostics on the line the stack trace points at, the quickfix list. The file toggle jumps between x.ts and x.test.ts (or x.spec.ts when that is the one there).
  • Code actions (vtsls): organize imports, add missing imports, fix all; go to the source definition (the .ts file instead of the .d.ts).
  • Packages: install, add (what you type: -D vitest), remove, outdated.
  • Setup (<leader>tS): tsconfig (strict Node/Bun, strict React with a bundler, a library that emits .d.ts), Biome (recommended or strict), ESLint flat config with typescript-eslint, Prettier, Vitest config (Node or React), a Dockerfile (Node multi-stage or Bun) and its .dockerignore, GitHub Actions (install, lint, typecheck, test, build with the project's package manager), .nvmrc, .editorconfig, a Makefile preset, .gitignore.

Snippets

describetest, ittest, zodschema (with its type), resulttype, asyncfunction, exhaustive (a switch the compiler checks); in TSX also component (typed props) and usehook.

Notes

  • Biome and Prettier never both format: Biome only runs where a biome.json is.
  • npx --no-install fails when the tool is not in node_modules: install it in the project first.

Languages · docs/cheatsheets/python.md

Python

Filetype: python. Pack python (installer checklist, or <leader>Sp → Languages).

Tools

WhatWith
Types, navigation, hoverbasedpyright (type checking "standard"; a pyproject.toml/pyrightconfig.json setting wins)
Lint, quick fixes, importsruff (its server; in light mode it runs as a linter instead)
Format on saveruff (organize imports, then format)
Debugdebugpy (Mason)
Treesitterpython, toml, requirements

The project's interpreter is used everywhere: a .venv/ or venv/ at the root (uv, python -m venv, poetry with in-project venvs) is basedpyright's interpreter, so the imports you installed resolve. A uv project (one with uv.lock) runs through uv run; otherwise the .venv python; otherwise python3. e in the menu shows which one is in use.

<leader>p menu (only in *.py)

The keys are in CHEATSHEET.md. What they do:

  • Run: the file, or a REPL with it loaded.
  • Tests in the editor (the same keys in every language with tests): the test under the cursor (a test_… function, a method of a Test… class, a whole Test… class), the file, the project, the last run again. pytest runs with a JUnit report: each test line gets a mark (✓ passed, ✗ failed, ○ skipped, … running), a failure is a diagnostic on the line of the test file where the traceback stops, the failures go to the quickfix list. Parametrized cases count as their function. From a module (not a test), the key runs its test file (tests/test_<name>.py). The file toggle jumps between a module and its test (creating tests/test_<name>.py when there is none). The output key shows what pytest printed.
  • Coverage: pytest --cov (needs pytest-cov).
  • uv: add packages (--dev pytest works: what you type goes after uv add), remove, sync, upgrade the lock.
  • ruff: check the project, fix what it can, organize imports in the file.
  • Debug (<leader>pD): the file, or the test under the cursor through pytest, with the project's interpreter; breakpoints, continue, steps and the panel, as for Dart.
  • Setup (<leader>pS): ruff config (standard or strict), pytest.ini, .python-version, a uv Dockerfile and its .dockerignore, GitHub Actions (uv: lockfile install, ruff, pytest), pre-commit hooks, .editorconfig, a Makefile preset, .gitignore. Filled from pyproject.toml (name, requires-python); never overwrites.

Snippets

testfunction, parametrize, fixture, dataclass (frozen, slots), ifmain, exception (with its data).

Pitfalls

  • basedpyright needs the dependencies installed in the .venv to resolve imports: uv sync first.
  • Tests from a class that does not start with Test are not collected by pytest, so they get no mark either.

Languages · docs/cheatsheets/rust.md

Rust

Filetype: rust. Pack rust.

Tools

WhatWith
LSPrust-analyzer with clippy as its check (lints as diagnostics, not only compiler errors), proc macros, inlay hints
Format on saverustfmt (from the toolchain: rustup component add rustfmt)
Debugcodelldb (Mason)
Treesitterrust, toml

<leader>r menu (only in *.rs)

The keys are in CHEATSHEET.md. What they do, at the crate root (Cargo.toml):

  • cargo: run, build, check, clippy (all targets), clippy --fix, add/remove crates (what you type: serde --features derive), update, doc --open.
  • Tests in the editor: the #[test] function under the cursor (#[tokio::test], #[rstest] too) or the mod around it, by its path (calc::tests::adds, --exact), the file's module, the crate, the last run again. Integration tests (tests/x.rs) run as their own target. Marks per test, a failure is a diagnostic on the line it panicked at with the assertion's message, the quickfix list. (Rust keeps unit tests in the file, so there is no file toggle.)
  • rust-analyzer: expand the macro under the cursor (in a float), open the crate's Cargo.toml, explain the error code of the line (rustc --explain E0382).
  • Debug (<leader>rD): the crate's binary after cargo build, or the test under the cursor (its test binary from cargo test --no-run, run with --exact); breakpoints, steps, the panel.
  • Setup (<leader>rS): rustfmt.toml, rust-toolchain.toml (stable + rustfmt, clippy, rust-src), clippy.toml, deny.toml (cargo-deny: advisories, licenses), a Dockerfile (Debian slim or distroless, dependencies cached) and its .dockerignore, GitHub Actions (fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests, cache), .editorconfig, a Makefile preset, .gitignore.

Snippets

testsmodule (#[cfg(test)] mod tests with use super::*), testfunction, errorenum (thiserror), implblock, tokiomain, derive.

Pitfalls

  • The first rust-analyzer start indexes the crate and its dependencies: the first diagnostics take a moment.
  • clippy as the check runs on save; on a very large workspace switch it back with a .neoconf.json ("rust-analyzer": { "check": { "command": "check" } }).

Languages · docs/cheatsheets/sql.md

SQL

Filetype: sql. Pack sql.

Tools

WhatWith
Completion of tables and columnssqls, connected to the project's database (below)
Format on savesql-formatter in the project's dialect (PostgreSQL, or SQLite when the connection is SQLite)
Runpsql / sqlite3 (they must be installed): no resident process
Treesittersql

Connections: found, never asked

In this order, without duplicates:

  1. settings.sql.connections ({ name = "local", url = "postgres://…" } or { name = "data", sqlite = "data/app.db" }).
  2. The project's .env, .env.local, .env.development: DATABASE_URL, then any *_DATABASE_URL, *_DB_URL, POSTGRES_URL, DB_URL.
  3. DATABASE_URL of the environment Neovim was started in.
  4. The SQLite files under the root (*.db, *.sqlite, *.sqlite3; not in node_modules, .git, target, .venv).

The first is used; <leader>Qc picks another and it is remembered per project (state dir). Passwords are never shown (postgres://app:•••@localhost/app). A config.yml at the root (sqls' own) still wins for completion.

<leader>Q menu (only in *.sql)

The keys are in CHEATSHEET.md. What they do:

  • Run the statement under the cursor (in visual mode: the selection), or the whole file. A statement ends at a ; outside strings, comments and dollar-quoted bodies ($$ … $$, as PostgreSQL functions are written). The result opens in one split (sql://results): the connection, how long it took, the rows as the client draws them (psql's table, sqlite3's boxes; ∅ for NULL); r there runs it again, q closes.
  • Asks before it hurts: a DELETE or UPDATE with no WHERE, DROP TABLE/SCHEMA, TRUNCATE, ALTER TABLE … DROP (settings.sql.confirm_risky).
  • Explain: the plan of the statement (EXPLAIN, SQLite's EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN); explain analyze runs it, so anything but a read asks first.
  • Schema: the tables (\dt+, .tables), the table under the cursor described (\d+, .schema).
  • Setup (<leader>QS): .sql-formatter.json (PostgreSQL dialect by default, from DATABASE_URL; dialect is the project's), a compose.yaml for the database, .editorconfig, GitHub Actions. Presets live in lua/langsetup/presets/sql/ and are filled from DATABASE_URL and the folder's name. Never overwrites.

Snippets

createtable (identity id, timestamps), createindex, upsert (on conflict … do update), withquery, transaction.

Pitfalls

  • psql reads ~/.pgpass and PG* variables as usual: a URL without a password works when those have it.
  • A long query stops after settings.sql.timeout_ms (30 s).

Languages · docs/cheatsheets/lua.md

Lua

Filetype: lua.

Tools

WhatWith
LSPlua_ls (NvChad enables it)
Format (on save and <leader>fm)stylua (always installed, also in light mode)
Treesitterlua, vim, vimdoc
Extralazydev: Neovim API types and completion of require() / plugin modules

Completion

In Lua the blink sources are: lazydev, LSP, path, snippets, buffer (lazydev with priority).

Useful commands

CommandAction
:luafile %run the current file
:lua =exprevaluate and print an expression
:Lazy syncinstall/update plugins (the git pull auto-reload does not do it)

Setup (<leader>lS)

stylua.toml, selene.toml, .luarc.json, .editorconfig, GitHub Actions (stylua --check + selene). Presets live in lua/langsetup/presets/lua/. Never overwrites: what is there is opened.

Auto-reload

After git pull, the .githooks/post-merge hook leaves a marker and, when nvim opens (UIEnter), core/autocmds/reload.lua runs luafile $MYVIMRC. Enable it once per clone:

git config core.hooksPath .githooks

It reloads Lua, but does not install/remove plugins or treesitter parsers.

Languages · docs/cheatsheets/markdown.md

Markdown

Filetype: markdown (*.md and *.mdx — MDX is detected as markdown, see ftdetect/mdx.lua).

Tools

WhatWith
Format (on save and <leader>fm)markdownlint-cli2 (fixes rules) and then prettier
Lintmarkdownlint
Renderrender-markdown.nvim OFF by default (plain source); <leader>mr toggles it
Treesittermarkdown

<leader>m menu (only in *.md and *.mdx)

MDX (*.mdx) is tolerant: no mdx parser exists in nvim-treesitter, so it reuses Markdown rendering, formatting and linting — JSX shows as HTML, tsx/jsx fences highlight, and imports/expressions stay as text instead of erroring.

The keys are in CHEATSHEET.md (generated; not duplicated here).

  • *.md/*.mdx open as normal editable buffers (plain source, no conceal). <leader>mr toggles the render, <leader>mz toggles the centered locked reading mode.
  • The diagnostics toggle hides the linter's diagnostics only in this buffer (the linter keeps running).
  • With spell checking on (es, en): ]s / [s jump between errors and z= suggests corrections (native Vim).
