TOML—prettier—treesitter only
Dockerfiledockerls and docker_compose_language_service——lazydocker available via TermExec
Prismaprismalsvia the LSP (lsp_format = "fallback")—no formatter in conform
YarnSpinner (*.yarn)——ysc (optional)see below

YarnSpinner

  • ftdetect/yarnspinner.vim: *.yarn → filetype yarnspinner.
  • syntax/yarnspinner.vim: highlighting for dialogue scripts.
  • The ysc linter is optional: it is only enabled if it is on the PATH (dotnet tool install -g yarn-spinner, requires dotnet). Error format: path(lnum,col): severity CODE: message.

Internals · AGENTS.md

Architecture

Neovim + NvChad v2.5 config — not an application. No CI or build system; the only automatic checks are scripts/gen-cheatsheet.sh --check and scripts/test-agent.sh (agent parsers and target, no network).

Repo structure

  • install.sh / install.ps1 — ONE-COMMAND installer (Linux/WSL/macOS · Windows; curl … | bash bootstraps by fetching the repo into ~/.local/share/leader, non-interactive by default, --ask prompts, --agent defaults to auto, plugins via Lazy! restore, per-step timings): dependencies, Neovim ≥ 0.11, nvim/ link, hooks, plugins, Mason and parsers; --lang a,b leaves only those languages (writes languages.disabled to lua/local.lua, without overwriting an existing one). Per-machine options: --lang, --theme, --light/--no-light (written to lua/local.lua by scripts/install-local.lua, ONE implementation shared by install.sh and install.ps1, never overwriting an existing local.lua) and --agent auto|backend/model|none (uses scripts/agent-setup.sh; skipped with --no-plugins). Uses scripts/install-mason.lua, scripts/install-treesitter.lua and install-local.lua; tests in scripts/test-install.sh; scripts/test.sh runs all the suites. VERSION is the config version (bumped with chore(release) + tag vX.Y.Z)
  • nvim/ — mirrors %LOCALAPPDATA%\nvim on Windows. Symlink it (or use the installer):
    New-Item -ItemType Junction -Path "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\nvim" -Target "C:\path\to\leader\nvim"
  • .agents/skills/caveman* — OpenCode skills (lockfile: skills-lock.json); managed from Leader by lua/skills/ + <leader>aK (see "Skills" below)
  • CHEATSHEET.md — GENERATED, don't edit: all the keys (leader menus, LSP, completion, nvim-tree, globals) read from the real keymaps by scripts/gen-cheatsheet.sh; the .githooks/pre-commit hook regenerates it if the commit touches nvim/. scripts/gen-cheatsheet.sh --check fails if it is stale. It is the ONLY source of keys: don't copy key tables into the docs
  • docs/cheatsheets/ — only what is NOT derived from code (what each thing does, workflows, tools, pitfalls): README.md + one page per language (go.md is the most detailed). When changing a language's menus, snippets or tools, update its page; no key tables (those come from the code)
  • scripts/ — gen-cheatsheet.sh (+ .lua): cheatsheet generator (headless nvim with the real config, ignores local.lua via NVIM_NO_LOCAL=1)
  • scripts/test-agent.sh (+ test-agent.lua, test-agent-backends.lua, fake-llm.py): inline agent tests (protocol, parsers, diff, target, local backends and setup against fake Ollama/OpenAI servers); no network or tokens
  • scripts/agent-setup.sh: setup of local backends and models from the terminal (report, --start, --pull, --auto, --use, --json)
  • .gitignore — excludes .claude/ (machine-specific Claude config)
  • app/ — the desktop app (leader, Rust workspace; app/install.sh, or ./install.sh --app). See "Desktop app" below

How it works

  • Plugins auto-install via lazy.nvim on first open
  • Activate the auto-reload hook once per clone: git config core.hooksPath .githooks (the hook picks the right data dir for Windows or Linux)
  • LSP servers and tools (gopls, vtsls, stylua, prettier, eslint_d, dart, etc.) auto-install via mason-tool-installer
  • No manual setup beyond the symlink (or ./install.sh, which also leaves plugins, tools and parsers already installed). A plugin with a heavy build that depends on a language must carry enabled according to languages.disabled (see plugins/languages/go.lua), otherwise --lang would install its tools anyway
  • Auto-reload: .githooks/post-merge creates a marker on git pull; core/autocmds/reload.lua detects it on UIEnter and runs luafile $MYVIMRC to reload config automatically

Architecture

One file = one responsibility. Minimal entry point in nvim/init.lua; everything else is modular:

nvim/
├── init.lua                  # bootstrap only (lazy + core)
└── lua/
    ├── core/                 # startup: settings, light, lazy, options, clipboard, theme
    │   ├── settings.lua      #   ALL configurable settings (+ local overrides)
    │   ├── keymaps/          #   cleanup (removes NvChad's) · general
    │   └── autocmds/         #   reload · treesitter · ui
    ├── languages/            # 1 file per language: DECLARES treesitter, lsp, mason, formatters, linters, menu
    │   ├── init.lua          #   registry: aggregates the declarations and serves them to each consumer
    │   └── actions/          #   menu actions (go, dart, typescript, python, rust, jvm...)
    ├── appearance/           # default theme vs terminal-inherited (Alacritty → base46)
    ├── themes/               # base46 user themes (terminal.lua is generated from the palette)
    ├── servers/              # per-LSP-server overrides (servers/<name>.lua) + loader
    ├── agent/                # inline agent (<leader>a): backends/ actions/ context/ output/ ui/ handoff/ (see below)
    ├── herdr/                # the workspace of agents (<leader>W): cli/ init/ panel/ report/ setup/ (see below)
    ├── menus/                # contextual leader menus (buffer-local) + Git context
    ├── plugins/<group>/      # 1 lazy spec per plugin: core lsp tools syntax languages navigation editing git ui
    ├── ui/                   # start/ (start screen) + dashboard.lua (its buttons), arts/ (braille art catalog), highlights, lualine, flat/ (the flat design)
    ├── utils/                # pure helpers: terminal, project, actions, git, log, telescope
    └── chadrc.lua            # only composes: theme + dashboard + NvChad ui

Adding a language: create lua/languages/<name>.lua (contract in languages/init.lua) and add it to NAMES. There is no need to touch mason, treesitter, conform, nvim-lint or LSP: they come from the registry. Overrides for a server: lua/servers/<server>.lua.

Adding a plugin: a file in lua/plugins/<group>/. New groups: add them to GROUPS in core/lazy.lua.

Adding a dashboard art: create lua/ui/arts/<name>.lua (returns the list of lines; braille-dot art only, U+2800–U+28FF, no ASCII/blocks). It is discovered automatically: ui/arts/init.lua scans the folder, with no list to maintain. It is chosen from the dashboard's "Change Art" button (ca); the choice is saved in stdpath("state")/dashboard.json. Initial value: settings.dashboard.art.

File operations (lua/files/, <leader>F; a/r/d inside nvim-tree use the same modals instead of nvim-tree's prompts, <leader>nd/nx are gone): acts on the row under the cursor in the tree, else on the current buffer's file (target.lua). New: one input, ; separates several, a trailing / makes a folder, the first file opens (from the tree, the tree closes first like <CR>). Rename: pre-filled name, a / in it moves the file. Move: a picker of the project's folders (ops.folders: depth 5, skips .git/node_modules/vendor/target/dist/build/.venv, capped) plus "new folder…". Duplicate: name_copy.ext, _copy2… Delete: confirm modal (folder: contents count; unsaved open files warned) then ops.trash moves it to stdpath("state")/trash/<stamp>-<name> with an index.json (30 days) and <leader>Fu restores the last one, refusing to overwrite. ops never overwrites and falls back to copy+delete across file systems (EXDEV). After a move buffers.renamed renames the SAME buffers: nvim_buf_set_name leaves a ghost buffer with the old name (deleted by its previous name, not the folder's), and the next :w says "File exists" unless the buffer is reloaded from the new place (edit!), so unsaved lines are saved before and put back after; LSP servers get workspace/willRenameFiles (edits applied before) and didRenameFiles. Tests: scripts/test-files.sh.

Settings panel (lua/prefs/',<leader>S):<leader>Spis one flat list (a telescope dropdown, the same widget as the agent model selector) of what is worth changing without editing a file. A group is a file inprefs/items/returning items (group,label,kindtoggle/choice/action,id= path incore.settings,applyfor the live effect,restart; contract at the top ofprefs/init.lua; new group = file +NAMES). Enter flips a toggle, steps through up to 3 values or opens a picker for more, and runs an action; the list reopens on the row you changed. A choice is written tocore.settingsin place (so every module that reads settings where it is used sees it at once) and saved byprefs/store.luainstdpath("state")/prefs.jsonas a flatpath → valuemap thatcore/settings.luamerges AFTERlocal.lua(and ignores underNVIM_NO_LOCAL, which keeps the cheatsheet machine-independent). A choice whosevalueis nil (light modeauto) removes the saved key. Items with their own persistence (diagnostic blocks,utils/diagblocks.luaget/set) have noidand read/write through their owner. Whatever is read once at startup (flat design, session autosave, light mode) is markedrestartand says so;conformreadsformat_on_savethrough a function so<leader>Sfis live.prefs/info.lua(purelines(facts)) andprefs/update.lua(git pull --ff-onlyon the clone, thenLazy restore; never merges). Tests:scripts/test-prefs.sh`.

Terminal-inherited theme (<leader>S): reads alacritty.toml (Linux, Windows or WSL under /mnt/*/Users/*/AppData), resolves its imports and generates a base46 theme (lua/themes/terminal.lua) with the terminal's exact background, interpolated grays and accents with minimum contrast (theme.terminal.min_contrast; never dimmed toward the background, comments = the terminal's bright black). The flat design is FAITHFUL to it (palette.from(..., { faithful = true })): the editor stays on the terminal's background (deep = bg, so the rebase is a no-op and Neovim and the terminal are one surface) and Visual is the terminal's own selection color; only floats/panels (soft) step away from it. It is remembered across sessions (state in stdpath("state")/appearance.json + a marker next to the base46 cache) and followed LIVE: while the active theme is the terminal one, a poll (theme.terminal.watch_ms, 1000 ms; 0 = off) stats the config files and recompiles when it detects a change (inotify doesn't work: on WSL the config lives on /mnt/c). With the default theme nothing runs. It also re-syncs when nvim opens and when focus returns. The compiled marker is SHARED by every open Neovim (it sits next to the shared base46 cache), so live updates (appearance.refresh, poll and FocusGained) compare against the signature THIS instance applied, never the marker: otherwise the first instance to recompile leaves all the others painted with the old palette. sync (marker) is only for startup. Startup cost: the marker also stores the parsed palette, the config path and its theme file; while the recorded files are untouched (alacritty.unchanged: only stat, memoized for 500 ms) initial, sync, refresh and alacritty.load use it instead of searching /mnt/*/Users/* and parsing TOML (on WSL that was ~60 ms per start and ~40 ms per focus return; now the terminal theme starts as fast as the default one). Custom path: theme.terminal.config.

Sessions: persistence.nvim saves on exit and core/autocmds/session.lua also saves in the background every session.autosave_ms (only if something changed and without closing your auxiliary windows), so a power cut doesn't lose the state; autosave_ms = 0 disables autosave. The session menus are in the Gotchas below.

Herdr (lua/herdr/, <leader>W menu, doc in docs/cheatsheets/herdr.md): Herdr is the terminal workspace that runs the coding agents; Leader talks to its CLI (herdr agent|workspace|pane|integration …, JSON over the local socket). One folder per piece: cli.lua (the ONLY place that knows the binary, the JSON and the timeouts), init.lua (the facade: agents/workspaces/pane/read/explain/focus/prompt/status/version, plus the registration of nvim as an agent), panel.lua (the breakdown: one row per agent grouped by workspace, its state, its transcript and WHY Herdr says so — utils/market.lua; it refreshes itself every herdr.observe_ms), report.lua (what THIS nvim is doing, told to Herdr: working while the agent runs, blocked while a proposal waits, idle otherwise — it hangs off agent/events.lua, the flow never knows), setup.lua (what herdr is on this machine and how to install/configure it). Settings in settings.herdr: enabled (auto = the binary decides), register (auto = inside Herdr), observe_ms, lines; the panel in prefs/items/herdr.lua. Pitfalls measured: herdr agent read REFUSES the scrollback of an agent that is working (agent_not_idle: "use --source visible"), so the panel falls back to the visible screen; the JSON error's message is what is shown (not the whole object); and the resume command of pane report-agent (the -- RESUME_COMMAND) only exists from 0.9.5 — 0.9.1 answers "unknown option: --", so the registration is tried with it and, if it is refused, again without it (the version is only a hint: a wrong guess never loses the registration).

Inline agent (lua/agent/, <leader>a menu, doc in docs/cheatsheets/agent.md): ONE-shot actions (implement, review, explain, ask, fix, document, refactor, hand off to Herdr) on the function under the cursor or the selection, with no chat or sidebar. One folder, one contract per piece: backends/<cli>.lua (build/parser; claude, opencode, ollama, openai (LM Studio/llama.cpp/vLLM) and command for CLIs declared in agent.custom), actions/<action>.lua (target, context, prompt, output), context/ (treesitter target + budgeted providers: rules, types the target uses (treesitter + LSP, any language), LSP signatures, diagnostics with their line, outline), output/<kind>.lua (presenters: replace = inline diff with accept/reject, findings = diagnostics, text = window with streaming), ui/ (spinner, preview, window, picker). Adding a backend/action/presenter/provider = one file + its NAMES list; the menu comes from the action registry. protocol.lua keeps the output format demanded of the model TOGETHER with its parser. Settings in settings.agent; the backend+model chosen with <leader>am are saved in stdpath("state")/agent.json. Local models and setup (backends/{http,ollama,openai,command}.lua, setup/): setup/detect.lua measures CLIs, servers (incl. the Windows host from WSL) and GPU/RAM in parallel without touching anything; recommend.lua (pure) proposes models by VRAM; actions.lua is the only thing that modifies (start ollama serve, /api/pull, remember endpoints, set backend/model); open it with <leader>am → Setup or scripts/agent-setup.sh. Ollama sizes num_ctx per request (otherwise it truncates silently) and light_backend leaves docs/explain on a local model without moving the main one. Everything loads lazily (the agent facade only on first use; lualine looks at package.loaded). Measured pitfalls: claude -p by default drags ~46k input tokens, with --setting-sources "" --tools "" --system-prompt ~600 (don't use --bare: it disables the OAuth login); the CLIs run in stdpath("cache")/agent (without the repo's AGENTS.md); opencode falls back to the default agent (with tools) if it can't find the requested one, opencode latency (measured, opencode-go/deepseek-v4.1-flash, one-line task): cold opencode run ~7-9 s and ~20k input tokens because the global MCP servers' schemas ride along (agent.opencode.mcp=false disables them by name, ~5 s and ~830 tokens); a private opencode serve + HTTP POST /session/:id/message (backends/opencode_server.lua, session created ahead, basic-auth password) is ~2 s; run --attach (v1.19) emits no text, so it is not used; the 2.x CLI (measured, 2.0.31) is detected by v2() in the same file and changes the shape (agents/system/permissions, variant in -m model#variant, run --standalone because the background service never sees the inline config, no --pure; warm server off until its v2 HTTP API is driven, ~3 s per action, and its JSON stream carries no usage events); a model in no_thinking gets #low (2.x defaults to max: 7.5M reasoning tokens measured against 7.5k on low) and 429/inference_failed/[overloaded] is retried with 10 s/20 s backoff even after a long hold; vim.system{cwd=} does not update $PWD and opencode resolves its project from it, so runner.lua sets PWD (otherwise the repo's AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md, ~10k tokens, leaked into every prompt); opencode run hangs waiting on an inherited stdin when called by hand without </dev/null; and its route could not be tested with network in the development environment (parser and command were, with synthetic events and scripts/test-agent.sh). .agent.md at the project root adds conventions to every prompt. DDD (lua/agent/ddd/, <leader>aD menu, a separate feature): local static analysis of layers, imports, naming and aggregates (port/implementation/wiring/use cases) that costs 0 tokens (diagnose: diagnostics, quickfix and panel), and LLM actions (implement, review, missing, ask) with their own registry (ddd/actions) that reuse the same pipeline and receive the <ddd> section (provider context/providers/ddd.lua). Only what is inside layer directories is read; settings in agent.ddd. Hexagonal architecture levels: one folder and one menu per level — agent/ddd/ (<leader>aD), agent/repository/ (<leader>aP: port, implement, review, check), agent/service/ (<leader>aU, use cases: implement, review, deps) —, each with its own action registry (same contract and pipeline as agent/actions) and a local 0-token analysis; what is shared (rg, type reading) lives in agent/discovery.lua; <leader>a is left for generic things. Project documents (agent/kit/, <leader>aS, not a level: it writes files, not code): AGENTS.md (model reads snapshot.collect: tree, languages, manifests, Makefile targets, scripts, README; A = 0-token template.lua), STACK.md (stack presets per category + principle presets appended by compose.set_principles + sections as a checklist: which ## to keep, compose.sections/filter_sections), DESIGN.md (design-system presets, or described with a named preset as <base>), SPEC.md (free text in draft.lua → requirements/acceptance criteria/open questions); sections fixed in compose.DOCS and checked on every preset by scripts/test-kit.sh; replace = confirm + files.ops.trash; new documents are linked from AGENTS.md "Read first"; c makes CLAUDE.md import @AGENTS.md. Reconcile (kit/reconcile.lua, <leader>aSj, 0 tokens, pure find + collect): contradictions and redundancies between the documents and the code with a preview of every fix (Enter applies: duplicate Stack rows, duplicate lines/headings, package manager vs lockfile, missing Read first links; report-only: tools STACK names the manifest lacks, folders no document mentions). Coverage stays for FR-n ↔ tests, tokens and folders. New stacks (Django, Flask, Rails API) and the Cache axis (Redis) widen tolerance; compose.dedupe_stack_rows/dedupe_lines avoid redundancies. Build with agent (agent/kit/build/, <leader>aSb + b all, t foundation, s domain, d design): phases ready → plan → review → write → check → fix → use → look → done. The checks are the manifest's own; pnpm's blocked dependency build scripts are approved in pnpm-workspace.yaml with the packages pnpm names (onlyBuiltDependencies on pnpm 10, allowBuilds on 11+, the placeholders pnpm 12 scaffolds set to true; verify.pnpm_allow_builds, 0 tokens; agent.kit.build.auto_build_scripts = false leaves it to the model); a missing Playwright browser is a small modal (Install / Don't install, the command shown): a no writes an ADR (<!-- waived: playwright -->), the check is waived (done, not failed) and later builds do not ask (agent.kit.build.install_browsers = false does not ask at all). Transport errors (ECONNRESET, 502/503/504) are retried twice when they fail within a minute; a request that already ran longer is reported, not repeated (Enter retries; a plan on a slow model says it is still waiting after 60 s and shows its size and 1200 s limit; a plan_backend that is not installed is named and the main selection is used). The plan gets a brief of the documents (SPEC requirements/interfaces/edge cases, STACK stack/layout/conventions, AGENTS conventions; DESIGN whole) and can use its own backend/model (agent.kit.build.plan_backend/plan_model, e.g. claude/sonnet) while files and fixes keep the main one; files and fixes get 600 s (a one-shot action has 120 s), and a cut-off fix resumes at the check, never by rewriting the file from the plan. Scopes (scope.lua, one table per scope: docs it reads/requires, plan rules, file-request line, existing/wiring/use flags): a layer (foundation from STACK.md, domain from SPEC.md, design from DESIGN.md) builds on the project's files (scope.existing, git's list minus documents/binaries/generated/dot folders and the scope's own previous files; <project> in the plan request; seeded into written so every file request sees their API, <current> for an existing file the plan changes); a need on an existing file is not a plan problem (plan.parse(text, existing, scope)); a changed existing file is existed (never in created(): never remembered, stale-trashed or cleaned); run and file list are saved per scope (state.path(root, scope), manifest_path(root, scope), "all" keeps the old names); foundation/domain skip the wiring check and the use phase. Tests: scripts/test-kit-scope.lua. Task scopes feature and recipe carry run.task ({id, title, text}); their plan/file/ask are functions of it (scope.rules(name, task)), they are additive (no file list, nothing stale, nothing of the project skipped), one saved run per scope (another task starts afresh). Feature (kit/feature.lua, aSbf): picker of SPEC.md's FR-n (coverage marks) or a new one (draft → prompts.feature JSON → pure feature.add writes it in SPEC.md's own style: heading/list, next id, edge cases, Qn questions; confirm; old SPEC.md to the trash); the use phase reads only the requirement (use_docs). Recipes (kit/recipes.lua, aSr, also in the library): presets/recipes/<group>/*.md with <!-- input: key | question --> lines and {{key}}/_snake/_camel/_pascal/_kebab placeholders; for: supports a+b and a+!b; tested: every recipe parses, fits a real stack preset, fills every placeholder. SPEC.md modules (kit/specmodules.lua, aSm, also in the library; aSm opens the full-screen marketplace kit/specmarket.lua (on utils/market.lua, which kit/addonmarket.lua — the customize step of aSt/aSd/aSk/aSK — shares; narrow terminals show one pane at a time; t/d open the STACK.md/DESIGN.md add-ons a module goes with; ★ = SPEC.md already mentions it): three floats (list, reading pane, strip of what SPEC.md gets), Space pick, ⏎ read and leave requirements out, p the text that would be added, A add after the usual confirmation that returns to it when cancelled; plan/restrict/added_lines are pure): presets/spec/<group>/*.md in SPEC.md's shape without ids (### Title, statement, Given/When/Then criteria; - [Title] … edge cases/questions; <!-- needs: … -->); pure apply gives each requirement the next FR-n through feature.add (document style), extras through feature.add_extras, skips a title SPEC.md has (extras go to that FR), marks <!-- module: key --> (idempotent; feature.block ends at such a line), resolve puts needs first; never removes. Tests: scripts/test-kit-specmodules.lua (every module: criteria shape, tags, needs, questions). Coverage (kit/coverage.lua, aSv, 0 tokens, pure checks + collect): FR-n ↔ tests (test text names the id, or saved plans' verifies), STACK names missing from the manifest, unused DESIGN color tokens (every spelling), folders no document mentions; ⏎ on an untested FR opens its feature build. Records (0 tokens, hooked into the build by record()): kit/adr.lua writes the plan's new decisions as docs/adr/NNNN-*.md + index when a review is accepted (and adr.summary sends every decision still in force to the next plan requests as <decisions> with plan.DECISIONS_RULE; a plan that reverses one writes Supersedes ADR NNNN: …, recorded in the new ADR's Status and as superseded by in the index, and the old one leaves the summary), kit/progress.lua rewrites PROGRESS.md (Now/Done/Open/Next, Done kept as a log) when a run settles; both linked from AGENTS.md via compose.link_file, excluded from scope.keep. snapshot.root() inside the build tab is the run's root. Tests: scripts/test-kit-records.lua, scripts/test-kit-feature.lua. Release kit (lua/release/, <leader>GR c/p/l): changelog.lua (pure: parse git log records, semver bump, grouped section, insert at the top replacing the same version, version from VERSION/package.json/pubspec/Cargo/pyproject), checklist.lua (pure: STACK.md quality gates (only local commands run: runnable), version/changelog, CI, shipping rows), init.lua (git reads, the flows; the PR description is one light request copied to the clipboard; never tags/pushes/posts). Tests: scripts/test-release.sh. Quality harness, each piece one file: clean.lua (pure: a model's answer as a file — fences with prose, echoed <file> tags, leaked tool-call markup such as DeepSeek's ｜DSML｜ and leading chatter go; problem() says what is still not a file and the request is asked again ONCE, prompt.again; a broken fix keeps the old file), wiring.lua (pure, 0 tokens: names the plan's exports promise that no other non-test file mentions; methods must appear as .Name (a style called Expression must not hide Calculator.Expression()), lists like Title, Muted styles split, framework hooks (Init/Update/View/String/Error, bubbles' ShortHelp/FullHelp…) excluded; behaviours fail the check, values only feed the use verdict), the plan's wiring rows (FR → user action → code → what they see) go into every file's cached system prompt, and use/ (the app driven as its user: launch.lua recognizes the kind from the manifest and code, never from the model; scenario.lua writes scenarios ONCE per build from SPEC/DESIGN — expectations never from the code — and every round replays them; drivers/ tui (private tmux server -L, -f own conf, the app under sh -c '…; printf "[exited with %s]"; sleep' because a dead pane loses its output; keys ONE at a time: a burst reaches Bubble Tea as one KeyMsg that no binding matches), cli, web (+ Electron; web_runner.cjs with the project's or the global Playwright, the server started detached and killed as a group), android (adb + uiautomator, needs a device you started); judge.lua free checks per frame + one verdict request whose free failures it can never overrule; async.lua runs a driver as a coroutine so the editor never blocks). Goldens are trashed before any use/look fix and looked at again (look_rounds, use_rounds). New driver = one file with available/prepare/play/finish + its line in use.DRIVERS. Fixes are edit blocks (edits.lua, pure: SEARCH/REPLACE applied line-wise, trailing spaces ignored, ambiguous or missing SEARCH refused; shrunk catches a fragment taken for the file; the one retry after failed blocks asks for the whole file). Every model JSON goes through clean.json (first { to last }, then %b{}: Lua's %b{} alone cuts at a } inside a string; vim.json.decode rejects an explicit nil opts); invalid scenarios or verdict JSON is asked again once. Free checks: verify.unhandled_actions (data-action|key|op|command… names, words of 3+ letters, that no script mentions), verify.drop_versions (invented Go pseudo-versions → go mod edit -droprequire), plan.generated (goldens, lockfiles, build output are never planned); not_see shorter than 3 characters is ignored (a keypad shows every digit). The verdict reads the wiring code (scenario.controls) and only the rows that changed per step (judge.delta); fix_round resets per repair cycle and live.user carries the use findings into the next diagnoses. Measured on calc-lab (opencode deepseek-v4.1-flash): see docs/cheatsheets/agent.md. Tests: scripts/test-kit-use.lua (run by scripts/test-kit.sh; tmux and Playwright parts run for real when installed). Tests (agent/tests/, <leader>aT: write/mock/fix + local gaps) and lenses (agent/lenses/, <leader>aL: security/performance/concurrency/errors/api, they come out as diagnostics with their origin) are two more levels with their own registry and menu. The pipeline recognizes action hooks: retarget (the result goes to ANOTHER file; the target becomes the end of the test file and the original is the subject the context comes from, with job.view so the spinner shows on the tested code), merge (the model returns only what is new, protocol.APPEND_RULES), reveal (opens the destination to the side) and prepare (prior work WITHOUT a model, e.g. running the test: if it passes, 0 tokens); lenses use gate (words: with none, they don't call the model). The findings of a lens only replace those with the same source. Adding a level = folder + registry + menus/agent_<level>.lua + line in menus/init.lua. Adapters (agent/repository/contracts/, providers contracts and schema, Repository-level actions in <leader>aP (port, implement, review, check)): if the target is an adapter (e.g. PostgresOrderRepository) the following are injected, with their path: the port it implements (by name or implements/impl X for/var _ X, searched with rg), the missing methods, the entity the port names, a sibling implementation as a style example and the real table (SQL/Prisma migrations) with dialect/driver/parameters; silent if the code is not an adapter; scope type = the type under the cursor or its receiver's. Persistent findings (agent/persist.lua + core/autocmds/agent.lua): those from review/ddd review/ddd diagnose are saved in stdpath("state")/agent-findings.json with their line's text and restored (re-anchored) when the file is opened; fix and ddd fix read them via the findings provider and, on accept, the resolved ones are removed. <leader>ax stop does NOT delete them; they are cleared one at a time with <leader>ac clear (cursor line or selection), with <leader>aC (file) or with :AgentClear [file|all]. Two models per backend (tiers main/light; docs and explain go to the light one, <leader>am → Light model); i/d/f open the inline instruction field (empty Enter = no instruction), a/R require it. Short menu: i d r e a f R g(handoff, Herdr only) h(history) m x l (plus c/C clear). Two-level fix: f = inline (target = the lines of the diagnostic under the cursor, scope diagnostic in context/target.lua; it carries <around> and, via the LSP's typeDefinition on the diagnostic's identifiers, the types they point to) and F = block (the whole function, same types). runs/ saves every run in stdpath("state")/agent-history.jsonl (prompt, response, tokens, cost, what you did with it) and <leader>ah shows it with a spend report, opened on the current project's runs (agent.history.scope, "project" by default; "all" for every project, or <C-f> → Project to read another one); claude -p runs with DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING=1 (the cache only charged writes: -40 % per call). The colors in agent/ui/highlights.lua are derived from the theme palette (no linking to Diff*/Comment) and recomputed on User NvThemeReload. Chat (lua/agent/chat/, <leader>aC, doc in docs/cheatsheets/agent.md): a persistent, backend-agnostic chat. The mini view is a REAL right split (settings.agent.chat.dock, default; dock = false brings back the old floating partition): window.lua opens botright vsplit from the largest editable window (editing_window, never a sidebar/float), a transcript split on top and the input split below, both winfixwidth/winfixheight; relayout keeps the fraction and the input height on resize, hide_all clears the fields BEFORE closing so the queued WinClosed cannot hide the panel twice. Esc/q only leave insert / do nothing; Ctrl-Q (or <leader>aC while the panel has the focus) hides. The transcript is icon-free and padded (a blank fold column + wrap margin, a row of air above the bar; a 3-row composer that grows to input_height; the user turn is a filled bubble with top/bottom air; ChatSection carries the faint uppercase section labels; markdown keeps source bullets/checkboxes and the language NAME, no glyphs). window.lua animates a Working… strip while it waits for the first token and a pulsing caret while text streams (chat.activity extmarks, 320 ms timer; stopped on hide/settle). apply.lua writes the complete files a Write answer returns (fenced blocks whose info string is language: path) to existing project files when the turn settles (set_running/activity; loaded unmodified buffers are edited in place, closed files are trashed as a backup; A on a message re-applies; settings.agent.chat.apply). Syntax: line marks use priority = 10 so treesitter (100) paints on top; display_fence shows language: path as language in the transcript (copy/apply keep the path) and infers bash for an unlabeled terminal block that starts with a common command; prompt.lua asks the model to tag every fence. An empty chat or the history home renders a plain-text reference of the commands, mentions and keys (render.lua's welcome block, home.lines). Session bookkeeping: PersistenceLoadPre hides the chat before a restore, gui/projects.lua hides it before only, manual utils.session.save hides it, and the autosave (core/autocmds/session.lua) waits while it is open because mksession would otherwise save its nofile splits as ghost file windows (the exit save closes them through close_auxiliary). Tests: scripts/test-chat.sh, scripts/test-chat-pure.sh.

Advanced menu: the batch features are not in the everyday <leader>a; actions flagged advanced = true (fix all a, fix from output o/O, plan p) and the proposal queue (] [ y l, labels from batch/queue.lua) are bound under <leader>aA by menus/agent_advanced.lua. A new batch-style action sets the flag and picks a key unused in THAT menu.

Skills (lua/skills/, <leader>aK menu, doc in docs/cheatsheets/skills.md): SKILL.md files the agents load on demand, managed from inside Leader (0-config: Leader infers from backend/model + project markers and suggests/installs without asking). One folder, one contract per piece: parse.lua (pure frontmatter name/description/autoinvoke), infer.lua (pure: small models get token-savers caveman, stack+env manifests map to go-pro/flutter-expert/dotnet-pro/aws-pro/gcp-pro/cloudflare-expert/netlify-expert/docker-pro/kubernetes-specialist/terraform-pro/…), store.lua (OpenCode discovery .agents/skills + .opencode/skills + .claude/skills global/project, skills-lock.json, create/edit/delete via files.ops.trash), catalog.lua (marketplaces: npx skills find/add/check when Node is there, HTTP catalogs, offline curated list; never runs a package manager without the user pressing install), adopt.lua (pure tolerance: legacy/already-worked repos — detected skills/agents/STACK/DESIGN/SPEC are conserved, invalid skills are listed but never announced, legacy adjustments adjust), market.lua (full-screen marketplace on utils/market.lua like aSm: installed/suggested/catalog, read with Esc/h going back, edit/delete/install all return to the same view, A applies every suggestion, summary shows Detected:), init.lua (facade + match_for_prompt/available_list). OpenCode gets the skill tool when skills.enabled (backends/opencode.lua); tool-less backends receive ONE relevant body as a context section (agent/context/providers/skills.lua, cap skills.max_chars). Settings in settings.skills; prefs in prefs/items/skills.lua. Tests: scripts/test-skills.sh.

Agentic layer (lua/agent/: events, guards/, budget, verify/, refine, batch/, fixall/, errors/, review/, plan/; doc in docs/cheatsheets/agent.md): features that hang off the inline agent's pipeline WITHOUT editing the flow they hang from (the open/closed rule of this folder). events.lua is a tiny pub/sub (proposal.accepting/accepted/dropped, job.settled; handlers run in pcall), features.lua is the composition root that wires listeners (verify) and is called when the pipeline loads. pipeline.run(action) resolves the target from the cursor and asks the instruction, then calls pipeline.launch(spec) (action, target, subject, selection, input, extra sections, meta, preapproved): every other feature launches through launch with a target it already knows. action.standalone = true skips target resolution and calls action.handler({ selection, action }) (fix all, fix from output, plan: all in the Advanced menu). guards/init.lua runs after the prompt is built and BEFORE the request: check(req) per guard (budget is the first; add one = file + NAMES) returns nil, block (notification, job denied) or confirm (one modal with every reason, utils.modal.confirm with opts.on_cancel so a "no" releases the job: runner.deny records nothing and emits job.settled denied). budget.lua is PURE (tokens ≈ chars / 3.5, price per token learned from the history of that model, caps day_usd/session_usd block, confirm_tokens/confirm_usd/warn_ratio ask); runs.call records one-shot calls (commit draft, staged review, plan: output = "oneshot") so the spend is complete, and runs/advice.lua (pure) feeds the report's Budget/Suggestions. Answer cache (cache/: pure core.lua = key/eligible/prune, init.lua = file in stdpath("state")/agent-cache.json): pipeline.launch asks cache.lookup(job) right after the prompt is built and BEFORE the guards; the key is the sha256 of backend+model+effort+system+prompt, so any change to code, context, diagnostics or instruction misses; a hit goes through runner.settle (the same path a model answer takes, 0 tokens, stats.line says cached) but is NOT recorded in runs/: it is not a model call, so history and spend report stay about real runs, and the cache keeps its own counters (cache.stats(): hits, tokens and cost saved, shown by <leader>aAk), a success stores in runner. Only text/findings outputs by default (settings.agent.cache.outputs, action.cache = true|false): a replace proposal is never reused. meta.fresh skips the lookup. Tests MUST NOT touch the real cache (K.fake() points it at a temp file), <leader>aAk shows its stats and clears it. Keys a hint advertises must work where the cursor is: the answer window (ui/float.lua) never takes focus, so float.enable_followup() binds the follow-up key on the code buffer while the answer is shown (released on close, WinClosed or cursor move; replace.rebind gives it back to a waiting proposal, and replace.unbind only deletes mappings whose desc says proposal). float.dismiss() is what the global <Esc> (core/keymaps/general.lua, after the diagnostic popup) calls to close the answer from the code (and to stop a call still streaming). Test: scripts/test-agent-keys.lua. Next edit (actions/next.lua, edits.lua, provider edits, scope window in context/target.lua, wired by core/autocmds/agent.lua: BufEnter baseline, InsertLeave/TextChanged record): edits.hunks/render are pure; check stops before any request when the ring is empty. Docs output is diff-only: actions/docs.lua sets merge = docplace.merge and asks for protocol.DOC_RULES (ONLY the comment); docplace.lua is pure (comment above the function replacing the old one, attributes stay between; Python's docstring goes into the body, replacing the old one; a model that retypes the whole function anyway wins as is). refactor stays whole-function on purpose: returning line ranges would make anchoring errors cost more than the output tokens saved. Code search is NOT part of the agent (lua/codesearch/, <leader>fs) but the agent can reuse its index: scopes.symbols(text, lang) returns { name, kind, first, last } for functions and types (an arrow function takes the name it is assigned to); index.build(root, cb) is incremental. Verify (verify/): accepting remembers the buffer's non-agent diagnostics (namespaces of findings and ddd excluded), accepted waits for the server to settle (settle.lua: no DiagnosticChanged for settle_ms, never past max_wait_ms; nothing heard = no verdict, no "verified" line), regressions.lua (pure) compares them as a multiset by source+code+message (not line), retry.lua turns the new ones into a batch of verify_fix (derived from fix_block; the attempt counter travels in job.meta.attempt), build.lua arms a one-shot BufWritePost that builds (go build, cargo check, py_compile or verify.command) because a compiler reads the disk, and blames only errors inside the accepted range ±2 (extmark anchors follow later edits). Refine (refine.lua): Tab on a proposal derives <action>_refine (task + "start from <previous_proposal>", instruction appended as priority), supersedes the old proposal (replace.supersede, its buffer never changed; proposal.raw is the model output BEFORE merge) and launches with the SAME target (anchor.range refreshed); Tab on an answer asks ask with a <conversation> section (meta.thread, oldest turns dropped past 4k chars). Batch (batch/init.lua): items {label, target, extra (table or fn(done_items)), input, diags}; ONE confirmation with the total (budget.check on overhead + targets; a cap blocks, confirm = false skips the question), items run sequentially each launched with preapproved = true and an extmark anchor re-read at launch (the range may have moved), progress in the action label (fix all 2/3, only when several) and in job.meta.hint (shown by the replace presenter), denied/cancelled abort the rest; batch/queue.lua moves through replace.pending() (step, accept_all via replace.accept_proposal(p, { keep_cursor }), list = quickfix). Its keys (<leader>aA], [, y, l) live in menus/agent_advanced.lua. Fix all groups LSP/linter/findings/DDD diagnostics by enclosing function, else type, else adjacent lines (fixall/group.lua, pure, bottom of the file first). Errors (errors/): parse.lua runs the output through getqflist({ lines, efm }) once per tool format and keeps the format that explains most lines (go, tsc, cargo, python, javac, generic; no buffers are left behind), source.lua reads the last terminal buffer, else the quickfix, <leader>aAO runs a command (tests/run.exec(..., { raw = true })); each batch piece gets only ITS errors as <build_errors>. Review staged (review/): diff.lua (pure) numbers the staged diff by new-file line, static.lua (pure) finds secrets/conflict markers/leftovers/sensitive paths in added lines (Lua patterns have no {n}: repeated classes), init.lua sends ONE numbered request only if real code remains, drops findings that do not point at a shown line, and places them with findings.put (open buffers) or persist.merge (the rest). Plans (plan/): locate.lua (references, completed with git grep when the server never leaves the starting file; collapse and rank pure) → prompt.lua (signatures only; validate drops invented files/symbols) → ui.lua (interactive panel through ui/panel opts.on_open/opts.hint) → steps.lua (each step is a plan_step piece whose context carries <plan> with THIS STEP marked and <earlier_steps> = what the previous steps proposed) → batch; snapshot.lua is git stash create. Tests: scripts/test-agent-{flow,batch,review,plan}.lua on scripts/agent-kit.lua (fake backend, temp project, canned modal answers, K.no_lsp() stubs vim.lsp.start: a real gopls would fire diagnostic events at random moments). Pitfalls met: modal.confirm's y must mark the answer before closing or WinClosed reads it as a "no"; commit-panel agent line chunks are trimmed from the END (fit(chunks, width, 3)), so what must stay visible is inserted at index 3; a timer callback can be queued twice before the first one closes it (ui/flat/fresh.lua checks is_closing).

Local FIM completion (lua/fim/, plugins/editing/llama.lua): optional ghost text from Qwen2.5-Coder 1.5B Q8 served by llama.cpp (llama-server --fim-qwen-1.5b-default, ~1.6 GB, downloaded on first start) on 127.0.0.1:8012. lua/fim/init.lua owns the server: M.ensure checks /health asynchronously, starts a detached llama-server with --ctx-size 8192 --n-gpu-layers 99 --sleep-idle-seconds 300 --no-webui, writes its PID in stdpath("state")/fim/server.pid, and M.apply(on) stops it/the plugin on off; the choice is saved with prefs.store (fim.enabled), toggled by <leader>SF, :Fim [on|off|status], or the settings panel's Editing group. plugins/editing/llama.lua is lazy (never loads when off): it sets g:llama_config with ring context reduced for 8 GB (n_prefix 128, n_suffix 32, n_predict 64, ring 8×32, debounce 150 ms), show_info=0, its instruction keymaps off, and uses Ctrl+Tab = full suggestion, Ctrl+Shift+Tab = first line. lua/fim binds those two keys globally (they do nothing without a hint) and llama.vim also maps them buffer-locally while a hint is shown, so they work in ANY insert buffer (code, chat, modals); Tab/Shift+Tab are left to blink.cmp and never accept the AI suggestion. The ghost text links to the flat LspInlayHint. The statusline chip is components.fim_status (FIM… while starting, FIM ready, nothing off). Per-machine opt-in: lua/local.lua (fim = { enabled = true }). Tests: scripts/test-terminal.sh/the real config load; the lifecycle and Ctrl+Tab accept were exercised against the real server.

Project tasks (lua/tasks/, <leader>R, menu menus/tasks.lua, doc in docs/cheatsheets/tasks.md): the targets of the project's Makefile in ONE picker, 0 tokens, nothing resident. It works with a Makefile and ONLY with a Makefile: every action (r l d a p o q s) calls tasks.need(dir) first, which says "no Makefile above … (<leader>Rc creates one from a preset)" and does nothing; there are no other runners on purpose (just, task, mise and package.json scripts were removed). make.lua finds the Makefile (GNUmakefile > makefile > Makefile, nearest at or above the current file's folder, never above the git root: util.stop, so the machine's ~/Makefile is not the project's) and lists its targets. Make cannot list its tasks, so two sources are put together: make -qp (database, runs nothing, exit 1 on purpose) gives the .PHONY targets after includes and .DEFAULT_GOAL (only trusted when the output says "GNU Make"), the Makefile's TEXT gives descriptions (target: ## text, or a comment right above); without GNU make the text alone is parsed. A task is a .PHONY target or a described one (with neither, every plain target). Pure parsers: parse_db, parse_file, build. init.lua caches per Makefile (mtime+size of it and its *.mk, <C-r> in the picker forces), sorts (default goal first, then the file's order) and owns the flows (pick, last, default = a bare make, ask, dry = make -n, quickfix, create). Targets run in the integrated terminal through utils/terminal.lua (exec.lua: pure line, busy = the shell has children, stop = Ctrl-C; running while another task is alive asks first, otherwise the line would be typed into the running process), so <leader>ao reads their output; <leader>Rq turns it into the quickfix with the agent's own errorformat parser. store.lua keeps the last target per project (and the last anywhere) as argv+cwd+arguments in stdpath("state")/tasks.json. Presets (presets/<language>/<preset>.mk, create.lua, <leader>Rc): plain Makefiles, one folder per language (go, python, rust, typescript, dart, java, kotlin, c, docker, shell; a folder dropped in is discovered, its label is in presets.LABELS). The first line is a # description comment (it stays in the created file), every target carries ## text (so the picker lists it) and every preset has a help target; recipe lines MUST use tabs. Creating asks language (the one the project looks like first: presets.guess from marker files, else the filetype) then preset with the file as preview, writes <git root>/Makefile and opens it; it never overwrites and does nothing when a Makefile exists above. scripts/test-tasks.sh runs a REAL make over every preset (make -qp must read it, .PHONY must include help, every listed target needs a description). settings.tasks.timeout_ms. Tests: scripts/test-tasks.sh.

.gitignore (lua/gitignore/, <leader>Gi, doc in docs/cheatsheets/gitignore.md): writes a .gitignore from what the project looks like, 0 tokens. Topics are plain files presets/<name>.gitignore (presets/init.lua: line 1 # Title: what it ignores is the section header, line 2 # detect: names globs folders/ always is not written; secrets first, base last). detect.lua (scan: names under the root, settings.git.ignore.depth/max, dependency/build folders counted but not walked; pure match/run), compose.lua (PURE: build adds only the rules existing lacks, never twice across topics, blocks with nothing new vanish with their comment; missing, rules), init.lua (checklist utils/modal.checklist with the detected topics checked and why; write never overwrites, appends; afterwards git ls-files -ci --exclude-standard warns about tracked files the rules now match). Rules that people commit on purpose stay out (no Cargo.lock, data/, secrets.* as a glob, .claude/); scripts/test-gitignore.sh checks with real git check-ignore that nothing a project needs is ignored by all topics together. utils/modal.lua got checklist (rows to toggle, Space/x/a, Enter) and an internal render so a modal can redraw.

Go project setup (lua/gosetup/, <leader>gS inside the Go menu, doc in docs/cheatsheets/go.md): config files for a Go project, one key each: golangci-lint v2 (minimal/standard/strict), Air (basic/api), Dockerfile (alpine/distroless), .dockerignore, GitHub Actions, lefthook, .editorconfig; Sp = checklist of all, Sm Makefile (tasks preset), Sg .gitignore. Presets are plain files presets/<topic>/<variant>.<ext> (line 1 # description stays, line 2 # target: path is not written); vars.lua fills {{module}} {{go}} {{main}} {{binary}} from go.mod and the real main package (pure module/go_version/main_dir/binary, collect reads the disk). init.lua owns TOPICS (key, label, default variant, alts that count as present, misnamed names the tool ignores: .golangcli.yml → confirm modal → rename through files.ops + files.buffers), never overwrites (an existing file is opened), previews the rendered file in the variant picker (tasks.picker.choose, files rendered into stdpath("cache")/gosetup-preview), and hints after creating (golangci v1 vs the v2 config, air/lefthook missing). The menu context got an optional groups = { S = "setup" } (which-key names of sub-prefixes, menus/init.lua). scripts/test-gosetup.sh has the real tools verify the presets (golangci-lint v2 config verify, air's keys vs air init, lefthook validate; skipped when absent).

Dart / Flutter (doc in docs/cheatsheets/dart-flutter.md): flutter-tools owns dartls and run/hot reload; in light mode plugins/languages/flutter.lua replaces flutter-tools.lsp.attach (its ftplugin starts dartls on every Dart buffer through it) so the commands stay and the server does not. Debugging is nvim-dap + nvim-dap-view (plugins/tools/dap.lua, lazy: flutter-tools requires dap), debugger.run_via_dap = true so breakpoints work in any run; never set debugger.register_configurations to something that empties dap.configurations.dart ("No launch configuration"). Code generation (lua/dartgen/): parse.lua is pure (build_runner ≥ 2.5 Starting build #N / Built with… / Failed to build… / E builder on file: + indented message and package:x/y.dart:L:C, and the old [INFO]/[SEVERE]), init.lua runs dart run build_runner watch|build per package (vim.system, stopped with SIGINT), errors → diagnostics in namespace dartgen on their file, statusline component components.dartgen_status (DartgenStatus/DartgenFailed), the missing-part note on BufReadPost; light mode builds once. Completion (utils/dartsmart.lua, blink source for dart): declarations for the stack read from pubspec.yaml (utils/dartheader.lua stack), named after the file; the import/part lines (dartheader.edits, shared with <leader>at) are applied in Source:execute after the snippet, never as additionalTextEdits (blink applies those after expanding it, and in a new file they land below the class). Neovim's snippet parser rejects nested placeholders. Generated files (utils/generated.lua): one list (*.g.dart, *.freezed.dart, protobuf…) used by the agent's types, code search, agent/discovery.lua and the pipeline (editing actions refuse there). Tests (lua/darttest/): parse.lua is pure over the JSON reporter (testStart/error/print/testDone/done; testWidgets reports flutter_test's own line, its place is root_line/root_url; the failing line comes from the stack trace frame in the test file; the loading … pseudo-test and non-JSON lines are skipped). init.lua finds the test under the cursor with treesitter (Dart has no call nodes: identifier + selector(argument_part); names joined with spaces = what --plain-name matches; interpolated names cannot be targeted), keeps results per file (marks in darttest.marks, diagnostics in darttest, sorted), quickfix and one note; a new run kills the running one. Run (lua/dartrun/): r/d/s/D all go through start(opts) (debug, mode, ask): device (devices.lua: flutter devices --machine → pure parse/usable/order/entries; the last one used, the only one, or a picker) → entry point (lib/main*.dart, last one first) → mode, then commands.run(opts, { cwd, target }, launch_config) with OUR launch configuration (launch_config, pure): -d <id> and --profile/--release go in toolArgs, NEVER in args: flutter's debug adapter only looks for the mode in toolArgs (profileMode), otherwise it starts the app --start-paused as if debugging. Choices are remembered per package in dartrun.json ({ target, device, device_name }; the first format, the target alone as a string, is still read). User setup_project configs win for r/d; a non-Flutter package goes back to flutter-tools. s/D stop a running app (stop_then waits for dap.session() to disappear) before the new one. state.lua is the statusline chip: info is what dartrun launched, whether the app runs is asked to flutter-tools each time; flutter's adapter 3.47 emits flutter.appStarted while flutter-tools waits for app.started (never comes), so started follows the adapter's event; the chip is refreshed explicitly (lualine.refresh) on debugger events. panels.lua hides the log, the dap-view panel and the outline (<leader>dH). A run is quiet: settings.flutter.log = "hidden" sets flutter-tools' log.shown = true so the log goes to a hidden buffer, and plugins/tools/dap.lua opens dap-view for a Dart session only when dartrun.wants_panel() (<leader>dd) or on stopped with a reason other than entry (the adapter stops once at the start). pub (lua/dartpub/): outdated + annotations are pure (direct/dev only, sections tracked by top-level keys). flutter gen-l10n on .arb save lives in dartgen (the plugin also loads on *.arb). Tests: scripts/test-flutter.sh (fake dart replaying real build_runner and dart test output in scripts/fixtures/), scripts/test-agent-dart.lua.

Start screen (lua/ui/start/, buttons in ui/dashboard.lua): replaces NvChad's nvdash with the landing page's start screen: braille art (aqua), repo and branch (yellow), user (faint), then buttons of a fixed width, one soft block each (label left, shortcut right), the selected one solid blue; j/k, arrows or Tab move, Enter or a click activates, and each button's own shortcut (ff fo fw th ch ca rs) jumps straight to it. layout.lua is pure (tested in scripts/test-flat.sh) and responsive: a short window drops the gaps between buttons, then the art. init.lua owns the buffers (filetype nvdash, which the rest of the config already recognizes): ONE INSTANCE PER WINDOW (selection, rows, saved options, cursor owner), so several panes can show a dashboard at once; opening a file in one replaces only that instance, and the window gets its normal options back (the GLOBAL values when the pane was split from another dashboard, whose local values it inherited; a normal window keeps its own local values). It hides the cursor while shown and redraws every instance on resize and when the art changes. To add a button, add it to actions() in ui/dashboard.lua.

Panes (lua/panes/, Alt+V / Alt+H, global <leader>V menu): a pane is a real Neovim window, not a layer. open(direction) splits (rightbelow vsplit, belowright split…) and opens the start screen in it (settings.panes.dashboard = false leaves the same buffer); dashboard, close (never the last; respects unsaved buffers), resize (settings.panes.resize_step), equal (wincmd =), rotate, swap (wincmd x), zoom (:tab split, the original layout stays in the other tab, vim.t.leader_zoom_from marks it) and pick (the pane list, tasks.picker.choose). preset(name) builds the typical layouts (double two columns, triple three columns, main a wide left pane plus two stacked right, quad 2x2; menu keys 2 3 m 4): it keeps the file you are editing in the first pane, opens start screens in the rest, and on a tab with several panes asks before replacing them (buffers stay open, unsaved work is never dropped; hidden is raised only around :only). only closes every other pane of the tab (<leader>VX) with the same hidden guard. editing_window() skips floats. Keymaps live in setup(), called from core/keymaps/general.lua after NvChad: Alt+V/Alt+H override the old NvChad terminal toggles in normal AND terminal mode (consistency); the vertical/horizontal terminals moved to <leader>Tv / <leader>Th (utils.terminal.new_shell(direction)). Alt+Left/Right/Up/Down = narrower/wider/shorter/taller; Alt+Z zoom, Alt+Q close, Alt+T the quick terminal pane (utils.terminal.pane: one unlisted shell per tab in a split below the current pane, Esc closes the window and Alt+T reopens the same hidden shell). The app's View menu has a Panes submenu. Tests: scripts/test-panes.sh (multi-instance dashboards, a file loading in its pane, resize, zoom, list, plain split, last pane, every preset's geometry).

Tree: plugins/navigation/nvim-tree.lua hides the project row on top (root_folder_label = false: <CR> on it only went up a folder, - does that) and colors names that start with a dot (highlight_hidden = "name" → NvimTreeHiddenFileHL/FolderHL in ui/flat/groups.lua, purple with the contrast kept on light themes); dotfiles are filtered out by default and H shows them.

Flat design (lua/ui/flat/, settings.ui): the whole editor looks like the landing page's demo editor, view by view. The editor itself is painted deep (the demo's editor; the theme's own bg is the "page" around it): init.lua applies the groups and then groups.rebase moves EVERY group base46 or a plugin painted with the theme bg onto deep (also fg = bg, how themes hide characters), so gutters, panels and plugin windows follow. A float, menu or panel is a FILLED BLOCK (soft), never a box: winborder = "solid" adds one blank cell of padding and FloatBorder is painted like the block, so the "border" is air. Where the demo has 2-8px gaps or paddings the terminal uses half cells: ▀/▄/▐ in FlatEdge (block over editor). Selection is solid blue with on text, matches and keys are yellow, groups purple, hints faint, additions/deletions are veils (blended over deep). palette.lua derives the tokens (bg, deep, soft, fg, dim, faint, on, accents) from the active base46 theme (or the terminal-inherited one), groups.lua turns them into highlight groups (pure, tested by scripts/test-flat.sh), init.lua re-applies on ColorScheme, NvThemeReload and every LazyLoad and owns the global options (winborder, fillchars, showtabline = 0, blinking cursor). The views: telescope.lua is telescope's create_layout (results over the prompt, preview right, titles INSIDE the blocks in faint capitals, the previewed path in yellow as the preview's winbar, the hit line veiled only when the entry has a line; geometry is pure); whichkey.lua replaces which-key's view.show (a full-width soft panel over the statusline, 4 columns filled by ROWS, groups first, faint Space » +Find … Esc close · BS back footer; grid is pure) and labels.lua hides the words the open menu already says (the demo ports it as trimLabel); grug.lua (soft panel, Grug FAR · g? help header, each input a deep bar with its aqua label); chips.lua (harpoon's chosen row as a chip as wide as its text); notify.lua (the demo's note: bottom right, a row of air above and below the text so the thin level bar runs the block's whole height (half-cell rows cannot carry it), the lead phrase bold in the level color, fades in, one row of editor between notes and above the statusline, stacks above Neovim's own message window); Neovim's messages use the built-in message UI (vim._core.ui2, settings.ui.messages): no "Press ENTER" screen; short ones ("file" written, errors, warnings, a print) are routed into the notes by wrapping its msg_show (notify.message: kind → color, progress steps coalesced into one note, a repeated note refreshes instead of stacking), listings/long output/prompts stay in its own windows styled as blocks, g< for the full pager; motion.lua (fade-in of which-key (plus a 2-row slide), modals, harpoon and notes, ~110ms ease-out; every frame flushes its own redraw because which-key waits for keys in a loop that does not redraw, an animation yields as soon as someone else moves its window, and full-screen surfaces (telescope, commit panel) never fade: blending them repaints the whole terminal; settings.ui.motion = false turns it off); fresh.lua (yank and agent accepts glow yellow and fade); keys.lua (the demo's keycaps, off by default, :FlatKeys). Elsewhere: modals (utils/modal.lua: title in fg capitals, tree glyphs, input as a deep bar), the start screen buttons (row + ▀ edge), the lualine theme (ui/lualine/theme.lua: soft bar, mode/position chips of fixed width), diagnostics blocks (message source 1/N, no icon or label: the bar and the veil say the severity; at the text column) and their line popup, blink (no blank rows, label … detail, docs half a cell apart), the terminal float (NvChad's Alt+i, chadrc.term: theme bg, the demo's place), Trouble (flat list, Diagnostics N header), Diffview (soft Changes panel, HEAD/WORKING TREE headers, no diagnostics in diffs) and the commit/agent panels (commit/highlights.lua, agent/ui/highlights.lua). Plugins take their border from require("ui.flat").border() ("solid", "float" compact, "beside" for a block glued right of another; "single" with ui.flat = false). New surfaces must take their colors from groups.lua, never hardcode a color or a border style. The landing page's theme picker (docs/index.html) ports palette.lua to JS: keep both derivations in sync. To compare a view with the demo, capture nvim through a terminal emulator (pty + pyte → HTML) rather than tmux, which drops erased cells' colors.

Per-machine settings: lua/local.lua (git-ignored) returns a partial table that is merged over core/settings.lua (theme, disabled languages, format-on-save, vim options, clipboard, light mode...). Example: return { languages = { disabled = { "astro" } } }.

Language packs and their shared layer

The installer asks one thing in a terminal: which packs to set up (nvim/lua/languages/packs.lua: one pack per line, read by install.sh with sed before nvim is installed, by install.ps1 and install-local.lua through --packs/--expand). The base (Lua, Markdown, JSON, YAML, TOML) is always on; packs whose tools are on PATH start checked; --lang (packs or languages, all, none), --yes or no terminal skip the question; an existing local.lua is never asked about again. Later, <leader>Sp → Languages toggles a pack, saved as languages.packs.<id> (one key per pack: the prefs are deep-merged and vim.tbl_deep_extend merges lists by index, so a saved list would mix with local.lua's) and it wins over languages.disabled (languages.disabled(name) in the registry). Only languages that are on install their Mason tools.

What every language menu reuses (one folder each, a contract each):

  • langtest/, tests inside the editor (Dart keeps darttest/, a streaming reporter). An adapter (adapters/pytest|jsunit|cargo|dotnet|swift.lua) says tests(buf) (treesitter: id, name, range, kind), command(ctx) (scope nearest/file/all, report path) and parse(out, ctx) (results with status, file, fail_line, message); init.lua runs one process and paints marks (✓ ✗ ○ …), diagnostics (langtest namespace), the quickfix list and a summary; results kept per file and repainted on BufReadPost. ids are what the runner prints (TestCalc::test_add, sum > adds, calc::tests::adds, CalcTests.testAdd); a result without the same id matches by a unique name. pytest via JUnit xunit1 (uv run in a uv project, else the .venv), vitest/jest JSON (--testLocationInResults), bun JUnit, cargo and swift text, dotnet via a TRX report (--logger trx; a theory's cases collapse into its method). Multibyte marks (✔ ✘) are matched with plain finds: a Lua pattern class reads bytes. Keys m M A . T O.
  • langsetup/, project setup presets (Go keeps gosetup/): langs/<lang>.lua (topics, root, vars(root) read from the manifests: package.json/pyproject/Cargo.toml/Package.swift/pubspec.yaml/pom.xml/build.gradle/astro.config/Dockerfile/.env/prisma/schema) + presets/<lang>/<topic>/<variant>.<ext> with # or // header lines; JSON, .nvmrc, .python-version, .swift-format, .fvmrc, .tool-versions are written without the description (langsetup.bare). Every registry language with a project file has its own entry (LANGS: typescript, python, rust, csharp, swift, dart, java, kotlin, astro, docker, lua, sql, prisma; Go via gosetup; HTML/CSS/Tailwind/JSON/YAML/TOML/Markdown/Make/YarnSpinner come with the language that owns the root). Menus: <leader>tS/pS/rS/cS/iS/dS/jS/kS/oS/DS/lS/QS/eS. Never overwrites. Keys under S (plus Sp several, Sm Makefile, Sg .gitignore).
  • langdebug/, nvim-dap per language, adapters registered on first use: debugpy (Mason's venv python, the project's interpreter), codelldb (cargo build/cargo test --no-run --message-format=json → the executable), netcoredbg (dotnet build → the assembly, ASP.NET's launchSettings URL), lldb-dap from the Swift toolchain. Keys under D.
  • C# / .NET (lua/utils/csharpproj.lua, csharpindex.lua, csharpsmart.lua, lua/langtest/adapters/dotnet.lua, languages/actions/csharp.lua): csharpproj finds the solution/project around a file (a solution wins), parses dotnet sln list, launchSettings.json, NuGet search/outdated/vulnerable JSON; csharpindex is a treesitter cache (types, bases, members, attributes, primary constructors) per file and per project (bin/obj/Migrations/generated skipped, unsaved buffer wins); csharpsmart is a blink source that offers ctors from fields, get-only properties, DbSet<Entity> in a DbContext, DI registrations (AddScoped<IFoo, Foo>) from the project's interface/class pairs and declarations named after the file, inserting the using each needs after the snippet; the test adapter reads TRX and filters by fully qualified name, with Foo.cs ↔ FooTests.cs for T. settings.csharp (and a C# prefs group) turns each piece on/off; servers/on_attach.lua applies Roslyn inlay hints. Tests: scripts/test-csharp.sh.
  • sqlrun/, SQL against PostgreSQL/SQLite with psql/sqlite3 (no resident process): connections.lua (settings, .env URL keys, the environment's DATABASE_URL, SQLite files; the pick remembered per project; sqls(root) feeds servers/sqls.lua), statement.lua (pure: ; outside strings, comments and $$ bodies; risky; reads for EXPLAIN ANALYZE), init.lua (results split sql://results, confirmations, explain, schema). The formatter's dialect follows the connection (formatter_options.sql_formatter.prepend_args).

Per language: Python (<leader>p: basedpyright "standard" with the .venv as interpreter, ruff server with hover off, uv add/remove/sync, coverage), TypeScript (<leader>t: biome only with a biome.json via require_cwd, else prettier; tsc --noEmit to quickfix; vtsls actions), Rust (<leader>r: rust-analyzer with clippy, expand macro, open Cargo.toml, rustc --explain), C# / .NET (<leader>c: Roslyn when installed, csharp-ls from Mason otherwise; CSharpier, dotnet/EF Core, netcoredbg, tests in the editor (TRX), utils/csharpsmart.lua + utils/csharpindex.lua for ctor/props/DbSet/DI completion from the project's own types and utils/csharpproj.lua for solutions, references and NuGet; setup presets), SQL (<leader>Q), Swift (<leader>i: SwiftPM, sourcekit-lsp only when on PATH), Make (treesitter + checkmake; tasks stay in <leader>R). Curated snippets per language in nvim/snippets/<ft>.json. Tests: scripts/test-langs.sh.
