Performance

  • git-blame is off by default — toggle with <leader>Gb (no BufRead overhead)
  • Telescope respects .gitignore in find_files (skip node_modules, dist/, etc.)
  • nvim-tree hides git-ignored dirs by default — toggle with I or git.ignore = false
  • Plugins are lazy-loaded via events, ft, cmd, or keys (not on startup)
  • Big files (lua/core/bigfile.lua, knobs in settings.bigfile): over max_bytes (1.5 MB), or a minified bundle (≥ 100 KB averaging ≥ 1000 bytes a line), the filetype becomes bigfile through a catch-all vim.filetype.add matcher, so nothing that follows the filetype starts (treesitter, LSP, ufo, lint, conform); swap, syntax, folds, completion are off for that buffer. A 4 MB Lua file opens in ~135 ms instead of ~1.9 s. utils/comments.lua folds long comments before the first paint only up to settings.comments.sync_max_lines (a synchronous parse costs ~2 µs a line); past that ufo does it asynchronously. Tests: scripts/test-bigfile.sh.
  • Benchmark (make bench, scripts/bench.sh [--runs N] [--app PATH] [--app-only] [--json]): the app's phases to the first frame, Neovim's startup, opening a 1 MB / 4 MB / plain-text file, the first picker of a session, the code-search index (first session vs stored) and the picker's file list; median, best and worst of N. Compare two binaries by running it with --app on each, interleaved: this machine's noise (WSL) is larger than most single changes. Measured on WSL2 (Mesa d3d12): first frame ~160–190 ms, Neovim ready at ~60 ms. LEADER_TIMING=1 prints gpu thread (ms) laps: creating the wgpu Instance (the driver loading) is ~90% of the GPU's startup (~75–100 ms), surface + adapter + device + pipeline together ~25 ms; the first present adds ~60 ms. Renderer::create_instance needs the display, not a window, so main starts it on its own thread right after the event loop is built (~15–30 ms off the first frame). Warm-up: the first real frame cost ~140–175 ms of main-thread work (22 ms rasterizing glyphs, 80–110 ms in the first submit: Mesa compiles the GL pipeline there, 40 ms in the first present). renderer::warm_up draws one throwaway quad into a 1x1 texture at the end of Renderer::new, on the GPU thread, while Neovim loads: the first frame then renders in ~50–60 ms. It was tried before the hold below existed and made things worse (the GPU thread was the long pole then); with the first frame gated on the config, the GPU thread has slack and it pays. Vulkan is not available on this WSL, so GL stays.
  • Saving a big file (lua/core/saveformat.lua, settings.format_on_save.async_over_lines, 3000): a save used to wait for the formatter, and that grows with the file (a 15k-line Lua file took 1.2–1.9 s with the language server as the fallback formatter; the write itself and every save hook together cost ~20 ms). Up to the limit the buffer is still formatted before the write (the file on disk is always formatted); past it the buffer is written at once (:w returns in ~20 ms) and conform formats it in the background (format_after_save), then writes it again. Both options are functions that read the settings at call time, so <leader>Sf still turns formatting off and on. Tests: scripts/test-bigfile.sh.
  • Linting is debounced (plugins/tools/nvim-lint.lua, settings.lint.debounce_ms, 250): a linter is a process (the Node ones cost 100–200 ms of CPU to start) and InsertLeave, saves and the format-after-save rewrite arrive in bursts; one trailing timer per buffer turns 25 events into 1 run. The first lint of a file (BufReadPost) stays immediate.
  • Start screen git info (utils/git.lua) is cached for 3 s per folder: the screen draws again on every resize and each draw ran three git processes.
  • Heavy-edit benchmark (make bench-edit, scripts/bench-edit.lua, real config in a Lua buffer with a UI attached): 15k-line paste ~10 ms, undo ~25 ms, copy ~18 ms, replace-all ~60 ms, a 300-notification burst ~5 ms, 5000-line save ~28 ms, reindent-all (gg=G, treesitter indent) ~800 ms, and per-key typing latency in a 15k-line buffer (p50 ~0.4 ms, p95 ~7–15 ms). Known limits that are Neovim's, not ours: the p95/p99 typing spikes come from the treesitter highlighter re-parsing a dense 15k-line buffer after each edit, and 5000 diagnostics in one buffer cost ~150 ms in core's underline/sign handlers (our DiagnosticChanged listeners take ≤2 ms; 500 diagnostics take ~16 ms). A frame is ~16 ms because present blocks on vsync (Fifo is the only mode on WSL's GL) and a single key reaches the screen in 15–18 ms, one vblank at 60 Hz, so the render loop is already at the display's floor.
  • Lua gets emmylua_ls when it is installed (lua/servers/lua.lua, settings.lsp.lua_server = "auto" | "emmylua_ls" | "lua_ls"; Mason installs emmylua_ls with the full pack, lua_ls stays as the fallback and servers/lua_ls.lua keeps its trimmed settings). lua_ls parses every file of the workspace and of its libraries (single-threaded, ~4 ms a file) before it can answer across files and keeps nothing between sessions; maxPreload has no effect and trimming its libraries saves ~0.3 s. The Rust server indexed this repo's 670 files in ~0.6 s on its own progress events: cross-file go-to-definition answered at ~0.7 s against ~3.8 s on the bare workspace (3 of 3 rounds, only the server under test allowed to start), and with this config (the runtime as a library, lazydev, a loaded startup) ~2.6 s against ~4.2–6.5 s, a steady ~2.5x in five interleaved rounds under heavy load (hover with the real signature, 181 completion items and the undefined-variable diagnostic on both). Its library must be the runtime's lua directory: the type stubs alone (vim/_meta) left hover at any, completion empty and vim.* unknown, which a check that only looks for a non-empty hover does not catch. lazydev supports it (supported_clients). Pause/resume act on servers.active, the servers chosen at startup, not the declared ones. A first attempt compared the two with a probe that searched a symbol also present in the open file and then with lua_ls still running next to emmylua_ls: both gave wrong answers (it looked slower), so a comparison needs only one server allowed to start and a symbol that exists in another file.
  • Language servers: stop, restart, pause (lua/utils/lspctl.lua, menu lua/menus/lsp.lua, <leader>L): s stops the servers of this buffer, S every server of every project, r restarts this buffer's, p pauses or resumes all of them (no server starts for any file until resumed), i says what is loading right now. A server's "Loading workspace" cannot be cached from here (lua_ls keeps nothing between sessions: ~0.8 s to start and ~2.8 s to parse this repo's nvim/), so this is the way out of waiting for one that is not worth it; the stop is forced and does not wait for the server (13 ms against the real lua_ls, in the middle of loading). Neovim 0.12's vim.lsp.get_clients() hides a server that has not answered initialize yet, which is exactly the one stuck starting (tsserver, jdtls, rust-analyzer on a big project), so lspctl.all() also probes client ids; and vim.lsp.enable(name, true) does not look at buffers that already exist, so a restart fires FileType for the buffer again. servers/lua_ls.lua turns off the third-party scan and the prompt, and skips files over 100 KB and past 2000 files (~0.3 s on this repo; the runtime library stays, for go-to-definition). Tests: scripts/test-lspctl.sh (a server that never answers).
  • Tests and scripts never touch your state (nvim/lua/plugins/editing/persistence.lua, scripts/hermetic.sh): Neovim saves a session on every exit, and a headless test that opened temp files in the repo replaced the repo's real session with files that no longer existed (restoring the project opened an empty buffer) and left ~1400 sessions of /tmp folders, 58 of 60 "recent files" projects and ~1000 agent-history lines behind. Two layers: persistence is stopped when no UI is attached when it loads (a script, a test, --headless; the terminal and the desktop app always have one, and a real terminal run still saves), and every test script sources scripts/hermetic.sh, which gives each run a throwaway XDG_STATE_HOME (sessions, recent files, agent history, shada, logs). Checked by running the whole suite against a snapshot of the real state: byte-identical before and after. New scripts that start the real config must source it.
  • Idle preload (lua/core/preload.lua, settings.preload): Telescope's modules load ~600 ms after startup, one small step per tick, never in light mode. The first <leader>ff shows results in ~37 ms instead of ~78 ms (the picker itself already streams: on 100k files the first rows appear before rg has listed them all).
  • Code search on disk (lua/codesearch/store.lua, settings.search.persist): the per-file symbols and their mtime:size stamps are kept as JSON in the state dir (one file per project, atomic, ignored when the format or folder differs; bump store.FORMAT when what index.read extracts changes). The first search of the first session builds the index (~5 s on this repo's 1.6k files); every later session loads it (~120 ms) and re-reads only what changed.
  • Startup order of the app (app.rs, platform::Cell): Neovim reads its config only once a UI attaches, so the app attaches before the window exists, with the grid computed from the last run's cell (display scale + cell size, ~/.local/state/leader/cell) and the saved window size; resumed resizes if the real grid differs (a maximized window, a new font) and a first run, which has no cell yet, attaches the old way. Neovim's config starts ~50 ms sooner. The config tells the app it will announce its first complete screen (leader_hold at gui.setup, leader_ready after gui.startup has opened the project or the picker, with a :redraw first so the content is on the wire before the notice), and the app draws nothing until then, so the first thing on the window is the real themed editor, not Neovim's black default. A 500 ms deadline (HOLD_MAX) covers a config that holds and never releases; a config that never holds (someone else's) is not delayed at all. LEADER_TIMING marks the first frame drawn after the config's notice as content frame (what the benchmark reports as content_frame: the real editor on screen; first_frame alone is not comparable between a build that draws black first and one that waits). Bench-only switches to measure what each piece buys on the same binary: BENCH_ENV="LEADER_NO_HOLD=1 LEADER_NO_WARM=1" make bench (ungated, no warm-up) vs the default; interleave the variants over several rounds. On this machine (WSL2, noisy, other agents running) the default reached the content ~25–75 ms sooner than the ungated build in 4 of 4 interleaved rounds (e.g. 195 vs 270 ms), and the window no longer opens on a black frame.
  • Reusing a window (app/.../single.rs, leader --reuse/-r or LEADER_REUSE=1, opt-in): every normal window listens on a private socket ($XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/leader.sock, else the state folder; LEADER_SOCKET overrides it; never in snapshot runs). leader --reuse file… sends its folder and paths and exits before building a window or starting Neovim (~6 ms against ~180 ms to a first frame); the window opens them through require("gui").open_external (a folder becomes the project, files are edited) and comes forward (request_user_attention where a compositor refuses focus). With no window listening it starts one as usual; "New window" in the app still spawns a separate process. The socket belongs to the most recently started window.
  • ftdetect/yarnspinner.vim — *.yarn → yarnspinner filetype
  • syntax/yarnspinner.vim — syntax highlighting for YarnSpinner dialogue scripts

Gotchas

  • Clipboard (WSL/SSH without DISPLAY): core/clipboard.lua uses OSC 52 to copy (Alacritty + herdr forward it); pasting from the system is Ctrl+Shift+V in the terminal
  • Undo, redo, clipboard, yank history (lua/history/, global <leader>u menu, knobs in settings.history and settings.clipboard.keep): undo.lua splits an insert into undo steps like VS Code (after whitespace, a new line, or a pause_ms pause) by feeding Vim's own <C-g>u from TextChangedI / a timer, ONLY when getchar(1) == 0 and no register is executing: with keys still queued (a macro, ., fast typing) the <C-g>u would land in normal mode as <C-g> + u and undo the replay. vim.o.undolevels = vim.o.undolevels does not break undo inside insert mode, so feeding is the only way; . still repeats the whole insert. u / <C-r> / U / <C-y> / <C-z> run normal! u/<C-r> silently and flash the '[..'] lines (ui.flat.fresh); <leader>uu is Neovim's built-in :Undotree (packadd nvim.undotree, moving in it moves the buffer), <leader>u[/] are :earlier 1f/:later 1f (the file at the previous/next save). yanks.lua keeps every TextYankPost newest first without duplicates, writes stdpath("state")/yanks.json on exit (merged with what another nvim saved; clear writes at once or the merge would bring entries back); p/P are wrapped to remember the paste so [y/]y can undo it and put an older/newer entry through a borrowed z register (restored). With clipboard.keep, x X c C are expr maps returning "_x… only for the default register (an explicit "ax still copies) and visual p returns P; they are mapped AFTER menus.setup() because loading which-key makes lazy delete the c stub of NvChad's spec, which would take our c with it. ui/flat/keys.lua ignores the fed <C-g>u. Tests: scripts/test-history.sh (types into a child nvim with nvim_input, one key at a time).
  • ysc (YarnSpinner linter) is optional: it is enabled only if ysc is on the PATH (dotnet tool install -g yarn-spinner, requires dotnet)
  • :checkhealth mason warns about missing ruby/gem/php/java/julia/composer/luarocks: it is informational only, no tool in the config needs them
  • Contextual leader menus (lua/menus/ + each language's menu field): each language has its own letter and only exists in buffers of that filetype (g Go, d Dart, r Rust, p Python, t TS/JS, c C#, m Markdown, s CSS, j Java, k Kotlin); <leader>G Git only inside a repo. To add a language: a new entry in the langs table. Don't define a mapping that is both an action and a prefix of others (e.g. <leader>h + <leader>h1): which-key resolves it after timeoutlen and closes the popup
  • Long comments (global <leader>C menu, utils/comments.lua): detects with treesitter (nodes comment, line_comment, block_comment... whichever exist in the grammar) blocks and runs of consecutive lines that are alone on their line, of comments.min_lines+ lines, and plugins/editing/ufo.lua adds them to nvim-ufo's folds with kind = "comment" (it doesn't duplicate those the provider already folds). Cc folds and saves the "folded" preference (global, stdpath("state")/comments.json; comments.auto_collapse is only the initial value), Ce unfolds and turns it off, Ct toggles the one under the cursor without touching it. With the preference on, ufo closes the folds of kind = "longcomment" (its own kind: treesitter's uses the node name and would also close the short ones) when each buffer is first shown: comments.sync_ufo adds/removes that kind in the LIVE list require("ufo.config").close_fold_kinds_for_ft.default; a BufWinEnter aligns the buffers that already existed and folds NOW (fold_now: synchronous :fold + close, before the first redraw) the newly opened buffers, because ufo's provider is asynchronous and let the opened file show for a few tens of ms; ufo then rebuilds the folds and closes them by kind without it being noticeable. Comments at the end of a line of code don't count. The closed fold's text (fold_text) adds the first line with content if the first one is just /**
  • Code folds (global <leader>z menu, utils/folds.lua, menus/folds.lua): closes folds by what the code IS, on top of nvim-ufo's folds. f/t/o fold the function / type / object literal under the cursor (pressing again walks outwards: closure → its function), b the innermost fold (zc, again = the one around it), m the methods of the type you are in (type stays open), F/T/O every function / type / object in the file, a outline only, e expand all, s focus (everything closed except what contains the cursor), u unfold here and inside (zO), z toggle (za), 1-3 fold everything nested deeper than that level (ufo.closeFoldsWith). Functions and types come from the textobjects queries (@function.outer, @class.outer, via agent/context/scopes.lua, same definition as af/ac); objects have no query, so utils/folds.lua matches EXACT node types (OBJECT: a substring match would catch argument lists and map_types): add a language's literal there. close() closes the range's own fold; if the line has none (one-liner, ufo not done yet) :foldclose would hit the PARENT fold, so that is undone and a manual fold is made instead (ufo may redraw it on the next change). Ranges are closed inner-first (later start first). Single-line nodes are never folded. The long-comment folds have their own menu (<leader>C); e here also opens them.
  • Conventional commits (lua/commit/, <leader>G menu: Gc panel, Gm panel + AI draft, GM automatic commit without a panel, Ga amend): ONE panel (commit/panel.lua, three stacked floats: files, an acwrite message with filetype gitcommit, and an agent status line below, because virt_lines_above on row 0 doesn't get drawn) with the three entry points of commit/init.lua. One folder, one responsibility: conventional.lua (PURE: split, parse_header, check for the live indicator, rewrite of type/scope, suggest_type/scopes, clean_message), git.lua (status with +/− per file via --numstat, stage/unstage, async commit, branch/upstream), ai.lua (prompt + describe, which announces in the panel what will be sent BEFORE spending anything), auto.lua (GM: no panel and in the background), highlights.lua (derives from the Agent* ones, which come from the theme), panel.lua (UI). The AI goes through require("agent").complete (agent/oneshot.lua: same backend/model as the actions, light tier reassignable with agent.tiers.commit, returns usage as the callback's 3rd argument); the draft is never requested on its own (except Gm) and replaces the message in an undoable way. The AI message ALWAYS comes out in English and on a single line (Conventional header; there is no language setting). It is fast on purpose: COMPACT diff (git.diff_for_ai: no git headers, -U1, no lockfiles/maps/snapshots, diff_max_chars cap split among files; --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/ forced, because a global diff.mnemonicPrefix writes c/ i/ w/ and the parser saw NO files: the draft lost the diff, same in agent/review/init.lua), the branch and the types; subjects of previous commits are NOT sent (the model copied their language; the scope vocabulary is computed locally). Measured with haiku: ~2 s and ~$0.0004. GM: if there are staged files it commits only those; otherwise it stages everything modified except git.commit.sensitive (.env, keys...), asks for the message, validates the header and commits; if anything fails (no backend, AI, invalid header, hook) it undoes what it staged and warns. The types and limits live in settings.git.commit and the .githooks/commit-msg hook repeats the type list (shell): keep them identical. Pitfall: the close by WinClosed arrives queued; compare with the current panel before closing or it will close the next one you open. Tests: scripts/test-commit.lua (temp repo, fake backend, whole panel).
  • Git remotes (lua/gitsync/, <leader>G menu: GP push, Gu pull --ff-only, GF fetch --all --prune): command is PURE (the first push sets the upstream; pull without one goes to origin <branch>), run reads the branch with commit.git and runs the line in the integrated terminal (credentials, SSH passphrases and hooks need a TTY; Esc closes the window and the command keeps running), and a detached HEAD / a repo with no commits is refused with a note. Tests: scripts/test-gitsync.sh (real push/pull/fetch against a local bare remote).
  • GitHub (lua/github/, <leader>Gh submenu inside a repo, doc in docs/cheatsheets/github.md): catalog.lua is PURE (the gh extensions: gh-dash/gh-observer/gh-enhance/gh-news, each with id/label/repo/cmd; installed parses gh extension list by repo OR command, launch, install_lines, items); init.lua checks gh, gh auth status and the repo where it matters, runs the TUIs with utils/terminal.tui (a named terminal: a running TUI is only focused, never typed into) and installs a missing extension only after a confirmation showing the exact gh extension install … && <cmd> (first launch does both); s is the checklist of missing/installed extensions. octo.nvim (plugins/git/octo.lua, lazy on :Octo, Telescope picker) gives the menu p/i/r (PRs, issues, review the current PR). Core commands (gh pr view --web, gh pr checks, gh browse, gh auth status/login) run in the shared terminal. Tests: scripts/test-github.sh (fake gh in PATH, no network).
  • Code documentation (utils/docgen.lua, <leader>C menu: Cd template, Cm missing): one entry per language in LANGS (where the comment goes and which lines) and, if "public" isn't "everything", in PUBLIC. Reuses utils/blocks.lua (blocks.list: functions/types without closures). export function/@decorator are captured wrapped: decl_of extracts the real declaration. Tag snippets (todo, fixme...) in snippets/all.json with ${LINE_COMMENT} (blink resolves it); todo-comments already highlights those tags.
  • Sessions (persistence.nvim, utils/session.lua, global <leader>P menu): saves buffers and windows on exit, one per folder (and per git branch; main/master use the base), and every session.autosave_ms in the background. A bare start inside a project (leader, no file arguments, a folder argument like leader <dir> is fine, a real UI and the home folder excluded) restores it automatically (session.auto_restore; session is set up before ui in core/autocmds/init.lua, so its VimEnter runs before the start screen). utils/session.auto_restore checks should_restore, finds the file itself (persistence's naming, branch read from .git/HEAD instead of a git subprocess) and sources it with load_file: persistence.nvim is NOT loaded on this path, no start screen is opened and thrown away and no notification is shown. Neovim enables filetype detection only AFTER VimEnter, so a session sourced there arrives with filetype empty and treesitter/LSP never start: load_file calls detect_filetypes (pure vim.filetype.match + set filetype, which fires FileType for the restored buffers). With nvim x nothing is restored or overwritten. The desktop app's gui/projects.lua already enters the project (it now uses session.file/load_file too), so the hook skips vim.g.leader_gui. terminal is in sessionoptions: terminals are no longer closed by close_auxiliary and come back as fresh shells in their windows (the tree, quickfix, help and outline still close before saving; restored terminals get Esc/Shift+Esc from attach_terminals). The dashboard's "Restore Session" button (rs) and <leader>Pr source it directly too; <leader>Ps picks a project, <leader>Pl the last one, <leader>Pw saves now, <leader>Px doesn't save on exit, <leader>Pq (and <leader>Q, except in SQL buffers where the language menu owns it) quits everything with confirm qa after asking about unsaved changes. Opening a single file (nvim x) and quitting still overwrites that folder's session: use <leader>Px if you don't want that. What it includes: options.sessionoptions in core/settings.lua. Persistent undo (undofile) is enabled in options; browse it with <leader>uu. Tests: scripts/test-session.sh (restore conditions, the direct file lookup with branches, close_auxiliary).
  • Autoread (core/autocmds/autoread.lua): autoread + checktime on FocusGained/TermClose/TermLeave reload what git or the formatters change outside nvim (never overwrites buffers with unsaved changes). Search with ignorecase+smartcase and inccommand=split (:s preview) in options
  • git-conflict.nvim: no default mappings (co/cb would shadow c operators); the actions live in the <leader>G menu (o ours, T theirs, B both, N none, n/p next/previous, l list in quickfix)
  • Go: struct tags and snippets: utils/gotags.lua is a blink.cmp source (go only) that derives the tag from the field NAME: in SentAt time.Time + backtick it suggests json:"sent_at" (+ ,omitempty, camelCase, yaml, db...); after json: or json:" it suggests the value and after , the options (omitempty, omitzero, string). Inside a tag the first item comes preselected (Enter accepts; outside it preselect stays off). With gorm: gorm:" suggests full values according to the field's name and TYPE (primaryKey;type:text, type:text;not null, autoCreateTime, foreignKey:XID...; no not null if it is a pointer) and after ; single options (separator ;, not ,; textEdit from the last ;); the backtick key menu also lists the gorm variants after json. utils/gomodel.lua (only *_model.go or model.go; gosmart calls it when starting a declaration and puts its items AT THE TOP of the list): functable → func (XModel) TableName() string (derived name: without Model, snake_case, plural), funcdomain (toDomain + fromDomain), functodomain, funcfromdomain (func (m NoteModel) toDomain() *note.Note / fromDomain(n *note.Note) NoteModel; domain NoteModel→note.Note; the constructor is looked up in the imported package and, if it isn't there, Reconstitute stays as a placeholder). With several structs: functable X, from<Base>Domain. Only for structs that don't have the function yet. For structs already written: <leader>ga (json snake_case), <leader>gA (+omitempty), <leader>gD (remove) via go.nvim/gomodifytags; also <leader>gs fill struct, <leader>ge if err, <leader>gT add test. Own snippets in nvim/snippets/go.json (VSCode format, blink reads them alongside friendly-snippets) with FULL NAMES, no acronyms, only for STATEMENTS and utilities (iferr, errorsis, waitgroup, contexttimeout, testtable, httphandlerjson...). utils/goindex.lua is the shared index (treesitter over text: interfaces, structs, methods, functions, constants per file, package and module; cached by mtime; the unsaved buffer replaces its file) for the 0-token helpers goiferr (if err with the function's returns; the menu opens by itself and Enter accepts it after x, err := …, vim.g.goiferr_preselect=false turns it off), gomap (mappers between XResponse/XRequest/XModel… and its counterpart in the module), gowire (the module's NewX constructors with arguments resolved by type/interface), goenum (IsValid/Parse/UnmarshalText/String), goport (interface from a struct's methods and var _ I = (*T)(nil)) and gohttp (handlers from a service; what changes per router —detection in go.mod, signature, route, query, body, response— lives in gohttpfw.lua), all called by gosmart (their items carry a sortText with ! to go on top) and tested in scripts/test-go-smart.lua. utils/goreconstitute.lua (typed recon…/funcrecon…, ≥ 5 letters: Reconstitute(all the fields) *X for each struct DECLARED IN THE CURRENT FILE (treesitter: type ( … ) blocks too; generic structs and other files of the package are left out) without one, no validation; ReconstituteX with several structs; gosmart calls it from declaration_items, outside extra_declaration_items because that one only runs for the func… family) utils/gopackage.lua (not a completion source: gosmart.quiet_lsp runs it on gopls' package X items at package …; pure decide from the folder's neighbours / cmd/ / main.go / module root, pure rank drops package main where it makes no sense and sets item.score_offset — blink honors it — so the folder's package leads; info reads the neighbours' first package line, skipping go:build ignore and comments) and utils/gorepository.lua (typed repo/repository ≥ 4 letters, ONLY when the file is in the domain layer by agent.ddd.layers.of on its path under the module root and is not *_model.go: the persistence port of each aggregate of the PACKAGE (struct with id/ID/<S>ID, not *Model|DTO|Request|Response|Row|Entity) that has no port; Repository when the package is named after the struct, else XRepository; methods = settings.go.repository.methods) and utils/gogetters.lua (accessors of the unexported fields of the structs declared in the current file: typed getters/funcgetters/accessors ≥ 5 letters at package level, and as per-method items + all getters after func (n *Note) through goimpl.method_position; receiver from the struct's other methods, initialisms id→ID, slices/maps returned as copies unless settings.go.getters.clone = false; goindex.file_structs() is the treesitter reader both it and goreconstitute use) and utils/goimpl.lua (treesitter, 0 tokens; gosmart calls it): the methods a struct is missing to satisfy its interface — after func (s *T) (automatic menu) and when starting a declaration —; the interface comes from var _ I = (*T)(nil), from the constructor that returns it, from the name (noteService ~ NoteService; also local module packages via go.mod) or from what is already implemented; the body calls the dependency in the same shape (return s.repo.FindByID(ctx, id)) or leaves a zero-value return; …all missing uses $N (nvim only supports one $0). Tests: scripts/test-go-impl.lua (scripts/test-go.sh). utils/gosmart.lua (treesitter) is the contextual source: when STARTING a declaration (func, type or New… at column 0) it offers func variants (simple, error, value, pointer, bool, result error, pointer error, ctx error, ctx result error, main, init), methods of EACH struct in the file (pointer, error, value, String), NewX(fields…) for structs without a constructor and type variants (struct, struct constructor, generic, interface*, enum string/iota, defined, func, error, options); after type Envelope → struct/interface/func; with the name of a package struct in VALUE position (x := Env, return Env, arguments) Enter inserts the literal ALREADY filled (Tab from value to value; in type position it doesn't apply); inside a literal Envelope{ } (struct of the same package) it opens the menu by itself on Enter and offers fill all fields + the missing fields with default values; inside interface { } → method signatures (method error, method ctx result error, embed...). Noise is silenced right there: no buffer words or the LSP's generic func/type, and statement snippets only show up inside functions (declaration ones, at package level).
  • desc convention in keymaps: 2+ words and the FIRST one is the section ("Harpoon add file", "Go run", "General save"). The dashboard's Mappings button (NvCheatsheet) and CHEATSHEET.md group by that word and discard single-word descs (the generator warns). The leader menus comply on their own (menus.desc prepends the 1st word of the menu name). Neovim's native keys are excluded by measuring nvim --clean; the native groups that still leak into NvCheatsheet go in excluded_groups (ui/dashboard.lua)
  • blink.cmp: Tab/Esc/jk (plugins/editing/blink.lua, utils/blinkkeys.lua, core/keymaps/general.lua): with the menu open Tab/S-Tab choose a suggestion; without a menu, inside a snippet they jump between placeholders. Esc and jk with the menu open ONLY close it (cancel: undoes auto_insert, returns to what was typed) and keep insert + snippet; without a menu they leave insert. Flow to keep what was typed in a placeholder: Esc and then Tab. Don't put the snippet jump BEFORE select_next in <Tab>: it prevents choosing suggestions inside a snippet
  • Treesitter builds parsers with the system C compiler (gcc) and requires the tree-sitter binary (tree-sitter-cli)
  • No standalone run: this is a config repo, must be symlinked to Neovim's config path
  • lazy-lock.json committed for reproducible plugin versions
  • .claude/settings.local.json is NOT committed (machine-specific permissions)
  • Astro formatting requires prettier-plugin-astro in the project's node_modules or installed globally — Mason only installs prettier itself
  • mini.surround keymaps: sa add, sd delete, sr replace surrounding delimiters
  • flash.nvim keymaps: s jump to any word label, S jump to treesitter nodes (preserves native r replace char)
  • telescope-fzf-native requires cmake installed on the system (auto-disabled if missing via enabled)
  • Auto-reload limitation: luafile $MYVIMRC reloads Lua config but does NOT install/remove plugins or treesitter parsers. For plugin changes, use :Lazy sync or restart nvim.
  • Light mode (nvim/lua/core/light.lua): auto-detects RAM ≤ 2GB and disables all resident stuff — LSP servers, go.nvim, golangci-lint (falls back to go vet). Mason installs only goimports+gofumpt. Keeps treesitter + conform (ephemeral formatters) + nvim-lint. Override with NVIM_LIGHT=1|0 env, settings.light.force or vim.g.light_mode.

Desktop app

app/ is a Cargo workspace (Rust 2024, deps pinned in app/Cargo.lock, cargo build --locked) that builds leader, a GPU front-end that runs nvim --embed with this config. Two crates, one direction of knowledge each:

  • leader-nvim (no window, no GPU, testable headless): rpc.rs msgpack-rpc on plain threads (requests block, notify never does; a Handler serves what Neovim asks the UI, a Waker wakes the event loop after each redraw batch), event.rs the typed redraw events, grid.rs one cell grid, screen.rs the whole UI state (grids, where win_pos / win_float_pos / msg_set_pos put them, highlights, mode, cursor, options) and layers() bottom to top.
  • leader-gui (the leader binary): app.rs winit's event loop, scene.rs screen → instanced quads on the CPU, renderer.rs wgpu (ONE pipeline, ONE draw call per frame, a shelf-packed glyph atlas that grows), shader.wgsl, font.rs (cosmic-text shaping with font fallback, swash rasterization, cell metrics), boxdraw.rs, input.rs (winit keys → Neovim key notation), clipboard.rs, platform.rs (WSL env, backends, saved window size).

Rules that keep it fast and correct:

  • Nothing runs between events: no render loop, no polling. A frame is drawn right on Neovim's flush (not through request_redraw, whose scheduling can add a frame), on resize/expose, and at cursor blink edges (ControlFlow::WaitUntil). Input is notify (fire and forget).
  • Startup overlaps: the font database loads on a thread from the first instruction, nvim --embed is spawned before the window exists, attach happens as soon as the cell size is known, and the GPU starts on its own thread while Neovim reads the config. Nothing is drawn before Neovim's first flush (no flash). LEADER_TIMING=1 prints every phase and input → flush → frame.
  • Before attach (Neovim reads no config until nvim_ui_attach) clipboard::SETUP_LUA sets vim.g.leader_gui and a vim.g.clipboard that calls back over RPC (leader_clipboard_get/set, served by arboard); core/clipboard.lua steps aside when vim.g.leader_gui is set, lua/gui/init.lua sets guifont from settings.gui.font unless the user chose one (Neovim's own default is a generic list, so "chosen" = differs from nvim_get_option_info2("guifont").default; the app ignores that default too) and publishes vim.g.leader_gui_font_weight from settings.gui.font_weight (the app reads it once in Ready: Fonts::weight, 400/500/… via cosmic-text; bold stays 700). linespace (Neovim's extra pixels between lines) is honored: Fonts::set_linespace keeps the glyph centered in a taller cell (font::spaced), the cell cache is rewritten with it, and a change only relayouts the grid; Fonts::small (the app bar) keeps its own compact metrics.
  • Grid 1 is stale under windows: with ext_multigrid Neovim leaves old cells on the default grid where windows now are, so only grid 1 may skip painting its default background; every window and float paints all of its cells.
  • Block elements and box drawing (U+2580–U+259F, U+2500–U+257F) are rectangles from boxdraw.rs, never font glyphs: the flat design's ▀/▄ half cells (FlatEdge) must tile with no seam. Braille and everything else comes from the font.
  • Cursor: shape and size from mode_info_set, colors from its attr_id (0 = reverse of the cell); blend = 100 on that highlight hides it (the start screen's NvStartCursor); blinking follows guicursor's blinkwait/on/off and restarts only when the cursor or mode changes.
  • App bar (appbar.rs): the tools on both sides are generic (left/right lists of { icon, label, tip, action, menu, active } from the config; a label draws a ▾ when it opens a menu, active keeps the block lit); every tool, the mark and the window controls show a tooltip after 550 ms under the pointer (a flat block under the bar, drawn over the editor); a right click anywhere on the bar but the controls asks the config to customize it. Its text is smaller than the editor's (font::SMALL, measured into Fonts::small and rasterized at that size: the glyph cache keys carry the size; scene::cell_text(..., small)), its height still counted in editor lines. The window has no system decorations; the bar is ours and part of the editor: it is painted with Neovim's default background (screen.colors.bg, so it follows a theme switch at once) and steps back (its content fades toward the background, 180 ms) while a modal is open: any float bigger than 12% of the editor (Telescope, the welcome screen, which-key's panel; notifications, keycaps and menus are too small). One layout() serves drawing and the mouse. Window controls: three small flat squares (no rounding, like everything flat), minimize · maximize · close on the right by default (controls = "left" moves them, close first), quiet until the group is hovered, then yellow/green/red with −/+/×. Left: Leader's mark (the brand's classicN from brand/dots.js, what its favicon uses at small sizes; antialiased discs, shader kind CIRCLE; on hover a wave of swelling, warming dots; a click opens the app menu), the project's name with ▾ (the projects menu), ⌂ and ↺ (each can be hidden: buttons = { mark, project, home, history }). Center: the current file's path, centered on the window while it fits, leading folders giving way to "…" (the last real file is kept while Telescope/the tree/a terminal has focus, nothing on the start screen). Right: the activity (newest LSP $/progress, lazy.nvim's install/update/sync/restore, mason-tool-installer's run, a save; a three-dot spinner, then a check for 1.6 s; a 2 px progress line along the bar's bottom edge, filled to the percentage or sweeping when unknown; fades in and out), then generic chips ({ text, fg, bg (= a pill), gap, action }) the CONFIG decides: lua/gui/init.lua sends only what the statusline does not show ("N to finish" from the install checks with action = "welcome", recording, gitsigns' added/changed/removed for the file, non-utf-8 encoding / non-unix line endings; LSP clients and the indent only when show.lsp/show.indent turn them on), with colors from ui.flat.palette.current() (mark = green), over rpcnotify(vim.g.leader_gui_channel, "leader_appbar", …), coalesced to one per tick and only when the JSON differs. Never repeat the statusline there (mode, branch, project, diagnostics, position). Clicks call the config by name: a path part → require("gui").click(i) (0-based, folders then the file: Telescope find_files in the folder, nvim-tree find_file for the file); the mark → action("menu", col) (the app menu), the project → action("projects", col), ⌂ → action("home") (the start screen, in a normal window), ↺ → action("history") (Telescope oldfiles), a chip with an action → that action; col is the editor column under the button (Bar::anchor), where its menu opens. The window shortcuts use the same actions (F10 menu, Ctrl+Shift+O projects, Ctrl+Shift+H/R/W) or the window itself (F11 fullscreen, Ctrl+Shift+Enter maximize, Ctrl+Shift+M minimize, Ctrl+Shift+N a new leader process); the config's menus ask the window for those by name with rpcnotify(chan, "leader_window", name) (fullscreen, maximize, minimize, new_window, zoom_in, zoom_out, zoom_reset; App::window_command).
  • Menus, palette and projects (lua/gui/menu.lua, lua/gui/picker.lua, lua/gui/projects.lua): a menu is a stack of flat floats (the root at row 0 under its button, a submenu beside its item; GuiMenu* groups, the chosen row whole: cursorlineopt = "line" because the config's is number; the cursor hidden with the start screen's NvStartCursor while any is open). Items: { label, keys | run | items, hint, detail, current, when } or { sep = true }; keys types the config's own mapping (so the item does exactly what the key does) and its hint is menu.describe(keys) ("Space f w", "Ctrl+Shift+R"); when hides it (Git only inside a repo); menu.visible drops doubled/edge separators; j/k skip separators, Enter/l/click run or open the submenu, h goes back, Esc/q or focus leaving every menu closes them. The app menu (gui.app_menu) follows Zed's: File, Edit, Selection, View, Go, Run, Git, Agent, Window, Help + palette, project picker, home, quit; every key in it must be a real keymap of the config (check with a nvim_get_keymap dump before adding one). The command palette (gui.palette, Ctrl+Shift+P) is Telescope over menu.flatten(app_menu) ("File › Save") plus every normal keymap whose desc has 2+ words (the config's own; Neovim's :help ones are skipped). Projects: every folder Neovim works in is remembered on VimEnter/DirChanged (stdpath("state")/leader-projects.json, newest first, 30 at most, never the home folder or /); projects.switch(dir) asks to save unsaved files (never drops them), saves the current session (persistence.nvim, auxiliary windows closed first), wipes the buffers, cds, remembers it and restores that folder's session or opens the start screen. The picker (picker.open, Ctrl+Shift+O) is utils/market.lua (every market lights the chosen row whole, cursorlineopt=line because the config's is number, with MarketSel and a ▌ MarketBar overlay in the MarketSel namespace, and hides the cursor block until it closes; spec.hjkl makes l/→ only move to the details and h/← back, so only ⏎ or a click opens a project): Recent, On this machine (fd's, else find's .git folders under the home, depth 5, in the background; caches, toolchains and hidden folders left out; fd prints folders with a trailing /, strip it before dirname), Start (path, new project = mkdir + git init, clone); the pane is drawn for any row (return nothing for titles), with text-only GuiPicker* groups because it sits on the editor's color; facts per folder (picker.info: stack from picker.MARKERS, the README's first lines, persistence's session file by its name from ONE listing per opening, then branch/changes/last commit from git status --porcelain --branch + git log -1 run in the background, ONE folder at a time: m.want is the newest asked for, so a fast sweep starts git only for the row it stops on; the pane says "reading git …" and is redrawn when the answer is for the row still under the cursor). Everything is cached on disk by gui/projcache.lua (stdpath("state")/leader-projects-cache.json, NEVER the file of gui/projects.lua's recent projects, leader-projects.json, which must stay a JSON list (projects.list ignores anything else); read once per session, written when the picker closes or a search ends): facts per folder with a stamp of three stats (the folder, .git/HEAD, .git/index; taken AFTER git answers, because git status rewrites the index) and the found list, searched again only when older than settings.gui.projects.rescan (1 h) or with R. Pinned projects (projects.pin/pinned, a pinned flag on the entry; trim drops the oldest unpinned past 30; a folder never opened can be pinned) come first in the picker (p) and in the projects menu (★). market.refresh(m, true) is the cursor-only path (list not redrawn: bar, pane and strip only); use it for moves and hovers. The app sends at most one hover in flight (hover_sent/hover_next in app.rs): the pointer's newest cell goes when Neovim has drawn the last one or after HOVER_WAIT (40 ms), so sweeping across a list is one hover per frame, not one per cell. The pointer never scrolls: utils/mouse.follow sets the window's scrolloff to 0 and keeps its top line (with the config's scrolloff 5 a hovered row near an edge scrolled the list, brought a new row under the still pointer, and the list ran away); Telescope's hover does the same on its results window.
  • Recent files per project (lua/recent/, <leader>fo, the start screen's Recent Files, the app's ↺ and Ctrl+Shift+R): not Neovim's global oldfiles. list.lua is pure (build(root, { store, oldfiles, open, exists, now, max }): open buffers first, then the store newest first, then oldfiles that lie inside the project, never git/node_modules/plugin files, gone files dropped, no repeats), store.lua keeps stdpath("state")/recent-files.json (project → { path, at }[]; a file is recorded under the git root above it, else under the folder you are in when it is inside it, else its own folder, so opening a file of another project never lands in this one's list; saved 5 s after a change and on exit; a file that is not a table is ignored), init.lua records on BufEnter/BufWritePost and is the Telescope picker (the project = git root of the working folder; <C-a> toggles every project, <C-x> forgets an entry). Listed buffers count loaded or not. settings.recent.max. Tests: scripts/test-recent.sh.
  • Browse folders (lua/gui/browse.lua, "Browse folders…" in the picker's Start group, key b, File menu and projects menu): Yazi-like on utils/market.lua: the folders of one directory on the left, what the one under the cursor holds on the right (subfolders and files, plus branch/commit/stack for a git project through picker.pane), h/←/Backspace up (the cursor lands on the folder you came from), l/→/click in, ⏎ opens it as the project (picker.enter_dir), o new window, p pin, . hidden, ~ home, / filter. It starts in the folder above the current project with the project selected. Listings are read once per visit (m.ls). A fresh picker cache must still be DRAWN when the picker opens (market.refresh after search): without it only the recent projects showed.
  • The hidden cursor has ONE owner list (utils/cursor.lua): the start screen, the menus, the full-screen lists ("nv": still shown while typing a filter) and the setup call cursor.hide(owner) / cursor.show(owner); the real guicursor is saved when the first owner hides it and restored when the last lets go, a value that already names NvStartCursor is never taken as the real one, and cursor.repair() (on BufEnter/WinEnter/ModeChanged) puts it right if nothing owns it. Never write vim.o.guicursor for hiding anywhere else: each page saving and restoring by itself left the cursor invisible when they overlapped (a list over the start screen).
  • Desktop packaging (app/package.sh, app/package/, app/assets/, packaging/; details for people in packaging/README.md): the application id is io.github.sazardev.Leader (Wayland app_id, .desktop file name, StartupWMClass, AppStream id: keep the four equal, scripts/test-package.sh checks it; platform::APP_ID); the icon name is leader. package/stage-tree.sh BINARY DEST VERSION is the ONE place that says what gets installed (binary, leader-setup with @VERSION@, the .desktop file with Exec=leader, hicolor icons, AppStream metadata whose <releases> come from CHANGELOG.md through metainfo-releases.py, man pages, license, this version's changelog); the tarball, the .deb, app/install.sh (which stages, then runs the package's install.sh --no-config with LEADER_LOCAL_BUILD=1) and the Arch PKGBUILDs all call it, so add an installed file THERE. package/install.sh copies bin/ and share/ into a prefix, rewrites Exec=/TryExec= to the absolute path, records every file in share/leader-editor/manifest (uninstall removes exactly those, plus what older versions put down, incl. the old leader.desktop). package.sh builds with cargo-zigbuild for glibc 2.31, then writes the tarball, a hand-built .deb (ar + two tarballs, no dpkg-deb; Depends: libc6, git, curl, Recommends: neovim (>= 0.11)), leader-editor-amd64.deb (same file, versionless name for stable links), SHA256SUMS and a detached .asc for each, signed with the key "Leader Releases" in ~/.gnupg-leader-releases (fingerprint 8E437A7936E48EAEDA88CD73A7DCC3DBB671476A, pinned in leader-installer.sh, in validpgpkeys of the -bin PKGBUILD and in packaging/README.md; no passphrase so a release signs without a prompt; its revocation certificate is in that folder). leader-installer.sh requires a signature made by exactly that key when gpg exists (a forged or missing one aborts; no gpg = checksum only, said out loud; LEADER_VERIFY=0 skips). The binary: no nvim on PATH → a notification and exit (a menu launch has no terminal); no Neovim config at all and leader-setup on PATH → it opens the window with +terminal leader-setup (someone else's config is left alone); the window icon is the embedded 128 px RGBA (assets/make-icons.sh: dotted N from 48 px, the solid N below). packaging/arch/{leader-editor-bin,leader-editor}/PKGBUILD and update.sh (after a release: version, checksums, .SRCINFO). Screenshots for the site and the store live in docs/screenshots/ (taken with a demo HOME of fictional projects, never the real one). Tests: scripts/test-package.sh.
  • Esc closes whatever is extra (lua/utils/escape.lua, the global normal-mode <Esc>): one ordered chain: the diagnostic popup and the agent's answer (never focused), then the window you are in when it is extra (classify, pure and tested: a float; a help/quickfix/panel/scratch split; an extra page that has a tab to itself closes the tab; never the last window or tab, never a modified buffer, the file tree only gives the focus back, market/nvdash/leadersetup keep their own Esc, a float with vim.w.leader_keep = true stays), then focusable floats left elsewhere (hover, preview; non-focusable overlays such as the notifier and keycaps stay), then back-after-gd and :noh. Telescope closes on the FIRST Esc also from insert (defaults.mappings), the setup closes on Esc (free unless an install runs; Backspace goes back a step). PANELS in that file is the list of filetypes that count as panels: add yours there. utils.market.new is atomic: if anything fails while it opens, the windows are closed, the cursor comes back and the error is shown (a page that cannot be closed is never left behind). Tests: scripts/test-escape.sh.
  • Speed rules for the agent and the UI: streaming answers are redrawn at most once per settings.agent.stream_ms (40) per job (runner.stream_soon; the final text is always drawn by done), ndjson walks its buffer once, Telescope's flat layout wipes its preview buffer with the window (one buffer used to leak per open), per-event handlers must stay under ~1 ms (BufEnter ≈ 0.8, CursorMoved 0.1, TextChanged ≈ 1 measured). Stress check used for this: open/close each UI 60 times and compare buffers, windows, autocmds and memory (all flat now; open+close takes 5–32 ms).
  • Updates (lua/update/,<leader>Su, the bar's badge, the logo's menu, Help › What's New / Check for Updates; knobs insettings.update):semver.luaandchangelog.luaare pure (versions compare as numbers, a pre-release sorts before its release; CHANGELOG.md is split on## [x.y.z] - dateand drawn as lines),source.lualooks GitHub up WITHOUT its API (rawVERSIONof the branch, the rawCHANGELOG.mdonly when a newer version exists, and the redirect ofreleases/latestfor the desktop app's published release; every request has a timeout and fails quietly;settings.update.base/releasespoint elsewhere, tests usefile://),store.luakeepsstdpath("state")/update.json(checked_at, latest, release, the changelog,skipped,announced),targets/<id>.luasay what is installed, what is newest and how to update (config= this clone:prefs.update.run{ done }→git pull --ff-only+Lazy restore;app= theleaderbinary, only inside the app: the release's installer in a terminal; a new target = a file + its id inupdate.TARGETS),init.luaisstatus(pure over the saved answer and the targets),check(async; skipped inside the interval,check_hours= 12, retried after 1 h when it failed; firesUser LeaderUpdateChecked; announces each version ONCE with a notification),skip,apply(a confirmation naming what will happen, targets one after the other, stops at the first that fails, then offers a restart in the app) andsetup(8 s after start, then hourly when due; never updates by itself).panel.luais the full-screen "What's new" (versions newer than yours ★ first, the changelog on the right,uupdate,rcheck now,sskip,oGitHub). The badge is theupdatechip (toolbar.CHIPS, actionupdate) and the logo's menu leads with★ Update to X…. Tests:scripts/test-update.sh`.
  • Install progress in numbers (lua/gui/progress.lua): a tracker over a Mason batch (the language tools): per package waiting/running/done/failed, time, bytes in its mason/staging/<name> folder and a smoothed speed, a "no data for Ns" warning after STALL (20 s), done of total, elapsed, a rough time left only after 3 have finished (never precise), a bar. Pure over an injected env (new, poll, finish, summary, describe, lines; tested with a fake Mason), begin_mason/end_mason wire it to the registry and MasonToolsStartingInstall/MasonToolsUpdateCompleted (a 1 s timer polls; started from ANY route: the bar's chip, the setup, the first-run installer). Shown in the app bar (7/9 · codelldb 12.7 MB 31 KB/s · 6m 40s, the progress line fills) and as the setup Install page's info block (a row per tool; Install is disabled and the status line counts while one runs; the page redraws on a 500 ms timer). At the end a notification says how many installed and which did not.
  • Long background work says how long (gui/init.lua activity): a running item keeps its first start (since); after 10 s the bar adds 2m 05s (M.elapsed), redrawn every 5 s by a timer that stops when nothing runs, and a gopls item running over 30 s adds that big projects take minutes the first time. Why: gopls's first "loading packages" of a Go project using cgo (go-sqlite3 through gcc) is a cold compile of minutes (go list -compiled=true -deps=true); the Go build cache makes the next load take about a second, so it is not hung. If a load never ends, look for the go list child of gopls (ps) and its gcc children.
  • Start and window (lua/gui/startup.lua, settings.gui.window, the settings panel's App group prefs/items/app.lua (items with when: listed only inside the app), the Window menu's submenus (pref_menu in gui/init.lua) and the setup's Window step, all writing the same prefs): startup.decide is pure (a folder argument → that project; files → nothing; started inside a git repo that is not ~ → that repo; else on_start: ask the picker over the start screen (default), last, home; nothing under the first launch's setup, whose Finish opens the picker). Entering goes through projects.enter(dir) (what switch does, also when Neovim is already in that folder). The window: state().window = { size, position, maximized } tells the app what to keep (appbar::Keep); the app tracks the size/place while the window is neither maximized nor fullscreen (note_normal, on Resized/Moved) and writes ~/.local/state/leader/window on exit (platform::Saved: line 1 W H or -, then pos X Y, max; old one-line files still read); a saved place is used only if it is still on a monitor. Wayland never reports a window's place: the app sets vim.g.leader_gui_can_position = false and the setup says so. The frame is gui.appbar.enabled (Leader's square bar vs the system's decorations, switched live). o asks the app for new_window:<dir>.
  • Mouse (lua/utils/mouse.lua, lua/gui/mouse.lua): hover is Neovim's own <MouseMove>: the app sends the pointer's moves (nvim_input_mouse("move", ""), once per cell, App::hover_nvim) only while mousemoveevent is on, which Neovim reports to the UI (option_set). The config turns the option on only while a buffer that asked for hover is focused (mouse.on(buf, { move, click, press, modes }) sets b:leader_hover; mouse.sync on BufEnter/WinEnter/FileType): anywhere else a waiting key sequence (which-key's leader menu, an operator) never receives a <MouseMove>, so never add hover to normal editing buffers. Handlers get getmousepos() and route by winid (one buffer's mappings serve every window of a menu stack or a market). Wired: gui/menu.lua (hover selects, an item with ▸ opens its submenu without taking the keyboard, click runs), utils/market.lua (every full-screen list: hover selects and refreshes the pane, click reads/folds, the summary's key chips are buttons: m.chips from mouse.chips of the hint row, a click feeds the chip's key into the list or pane), utils/modal.lua (button chips found while rendering, BUTTONS[buf]; the one under the pointer takes the primary color; a click feeds its key, in normal and insert mode for the input modal; the checklist's items follow the pointer and toggle on click through hooks), the welcome screen, the start screen (ui/start), Telescope's prompt (gui/mouse.telescope, mapped after Telescope's own: hover = set_selection, press on a result = select and open, press outside its windows = close) and nvim-tree (hover follows, one click opens). Clicks work in a terminal too (mouse=a); hover only in the app. The app shows an arrow over floats and an I-beam over text. Tests: stub vim.fn.getmousepos and call the buffer mapping's callback (scripts/test-gui.lua), the protocol in tests/embed.rs.
  • Guided setup (lua/gui/wizard.lua, when it opens: lua/gui/onboarding.lua; checks: lua/gui/checks.lua; system packages: lua/gui/sysdeps.lua; saved state: lua/gui/state.lua, stdpath("state")/leader-gui.json): its own full-screen float (not a market), one wizard at a time (wizard.current); while it is open the statusline and tabline are hidden and the cursor block too (NvStartCursor), all restored on close. The page has a fixed layout that never scrolls: the steps across the top (✓ done and clickable, the current one lit, ○ ahead; only numbers when the window is narrow), the title and what to do, the choices flowing into up to three columns, and at the bottom a status line (✓ and what was chosen, or ● what is still missing), the buttons (Back on the left, the step's own and Next on the right) and the keys. Each step has ready(w) → ok, what is missing: Next stays disabled until then (a theme picked; a language or Only the basics; a model, an agent to install or No agent for now; Install done or Skip for now). Drawn on a small canvas (segments by display column, fill paints a background under text through a column → byte map; every clickable thing leaves a region, the whole cell for a choice; widths must be integers or the extmarks fail). Keys: arrows/hjkl move, Space chooses, Enter chooses then goes on (the primary button in Install/Review), Backspace back, Tab to the buttons, Esc/q leave (free unless an install runs); the mouse through utils/mouse (hover focuses, click chooses, steps and buttons click). API for tests: open(opts) → w (w.items, w.buttons, w.regions), go, next, choose(w, index), close. Opens by itself only on the first launch (1.2 s after VimEnter); afterwards Ctrl+Shift+W, Help › Welcome and the bar's "to finish" chip open it (on Review once finished). Choices apply as they are made and are saved where the settings panel saves them: theme through appearance.use (previewed when the focus rests on a theme, 110 ms debounce), packs as languages.packs.<id> in prefs/store when you leave the step (they start after a restart: the Review offers "Restart Leader" = the app's restart:<dir> window command, a new window then confirm qall), features through prefs.cycle (their ids in wizard.FEATURES), the agent through agent.selection.set. Install: sysdeps is pure (tools with their package per manager, manager() in pacman > apt > dnf > zypper > apk > brew order, command() with sudo unless root/brew and apt's update first, missing() with pack runtimes only for packs that are on); the plan = missing system tools + failing checks that carry a fix + the agents asked for (wizard.AGENT_INSTALL); nothing runs before the Install button; the system part (and agent installers) runs as ONE shell command in a floating terminal over the wizard (zindex 250, jobstart(..., { term = true }), the command printed first, sudo prompts there; it closes itself on success, on failure its title says so and q goes back), then Leader's fixes run and their status comes from re-running the checks every 1.5 s; a 120 ms timer animates the spinners while anything runs. Never run a package manager without the user pressing Install, never hide the command.
  • Toolbar (lua/gui/toolbar.lua): the catalog TOOLS (id, side, on by default, icon, optional label, tooltip with its key, action, active for toggles like the tree) and CHIPS; your on/off choices live in gui/state (toolbar = { id = bool, ["chip.<id>"] = bool }) and win over settings.gui.appbar.buttons / show. toolbar.customize is a modal.checklist (groups Left of the path / Right of the path / Information); a right click on the bar sends action("customize"). Actions: theme (gui.theme_menu: items with preview that the menu runs on hover/cursor after 90 ms; opts.revert undoes them when the menus close without a choice), agent (gui.agent_menu: available backends as submenus of their models, the current one dotted, then <leader>am, agent setup, the wizard), tree, terminal, problems, settings, customize, palette, home, history, projects.
  • Colors are blended in sRGB like a terminal (non-sRGB surface when there is one; otherwise the shader converts).
  • WSL: platform::prepare_env fills WAYLAND_DISPLAY/DISPLAY from WSLg and sets GALLIUM_DRIVER=d3d12 (the Windows GPU through /dev/dxg; without it Mesa falls back to llvmpipe) before anything starts; WSL has no Vulkan driver, so the backend is GL there (Vulkan + GL elsewhere, WGPU_BACKEND overrides). WSLg's GL only offers Fifo presentation, so a frame waits for vsync there.
  • Website (docs/, live at https://leader.sazardev.stream): Cloudflare Pages project leader (direct upload: npx wrangler pages deploy docs --project-name leader --branch=master; the custom domain is leader.sazardev.stream, a proxied CNAME to leader-e6o.pages.dev). docs/_redirects defines the short links: /install serves docs/install.sh (the one-liner: on Linux x86_64 it installs the app and the config, elsewhere the config; options are routed to the right installer), /deb and /app point at the latest release assets, /install.ps1 at the Windows bootstrap. docs/_headers adds the security and cache headers, docs/404.html the not-found page, robots.txt + sitemap.xml + llms.txt the crawler surface, and every page carries canonical URLs, Open Graph/Twitter tags and JSON-LD for the domain. docs/docs/index.html is generated by scripts/gen-docs.sh (FAQ included); the landing page (docs/index.html) is hand-written. docs/offline.html is the same guide as ONE file (scripts/gen-offline.sh inlines the fonts and icons, noindex, canonical to /docs/; the hook and CI check it), linked from the site and the README. docs/assets/banner.html + social-preview.html render the README banner and the GitHub social preview (docs/assets/README.md); the social preview is uploaded by hand (no API). The HTML-only <!-- docs:skip --> block in README (banner + badges) is stripped from the generated docs page. IndexNow is submitted after a deploy with the key file at the root.
  • Community and CI (root + .github/): CONTRIBUTING.md (setup, tests, commit/PR rules), CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md, SECURITY.md (private advisories, scope, security model and the unsigned 1.15–1.18 assets), SUPPORT.md, GOVERNANCE.md, ROADMAP.md, AUTHORS.md and docs/FAQ.md. .github/ has the issue forms (bug/feature, blank issues off, links to Discussions/docs/security), the PR template, CODEOWNERS, Dependabot (Cargo in app/, GitHub Actions), and workflows/ci.yml: generated docs + offline guide checks, the hermetic suites (session prefs release panes terminal escape chat-pure flat bigfile history) and cargo test --locked --workspace with Neovim installed. Repository settings live in GitHub, not in the repo: Discussions on, wiki/projects off, squash-only + auto-merge + delete branch, Dependabot alerts/updates and private vulnerability reporting on.
  • Release package (app/package.sh): cargo zigbuild for x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.2.31 (the binary needs glibc ≤ 2.30 symbols and links only libc/libm/libdl/pthread; Wayland, X11, Vulkan and GL are dlopened), then app/dist/leader-linux-x86_64.tar.gz (+ .sha256: bin/leader, the menu entry and icon, app/package/install.sh, VERSION, the version's CHANGELOG section, a README) and leader-installer.sh (app/package/leader-installer.sh: downloads the latest release's package, checks the SHA-256, runs its installer). The package's installer is guided and asks nothing in a pipe: checks the machine (Linux x86_64, glibc 2.31+, a desktop or WSLg), installs the app, then Neovim and the config through the main install.sh (fetched from master) when they are missing or older than the app (no lua/gui/), and offers to open Leader (its first launch is the welcome screen). Check a package in a clean ubuntu:20.04 container (LEADER_RELEASE_URL=file:///dist) before uploading. A release = chore(release): vX.Y.Z (VERSION + both crates' versions + CHANGELOG) + tag, then gh release create with the package, its checksum, the bootstrap and screenshots (taken with a demo HOME, never one listing private projects), through GH_CONFIG_DIR=~/.config/gh-personal.
  • Tests: scripts/test-app.sh (unit tests + tests/embed.rs against a real nvim --embed --clean: windows, floats, resize, Neovim calling the UI back; skipped without cargo). --gui also opens the real window: LEADER_SNAPSHOT=out.png draws the frame offscreen to a PNG after LEADER_SNAPSHOT_MS (default 1500), optionally after typing LEADER_KEYS, then quits; use it to look at a change (LEADER_KEYS=" ff" = Telescope, " " = which-key). To try the worktree's config instead of the linked one: XDG_CONFIG_HOME=/tmp/x with /tmp/x/nvim → this nvim/ (plugins stay shared).
  • Not done yet: ligatures, IME composition (dead keys and AltGr work; AltGr that arrives as Ctrl+Alt types its character), smooth scrolling, native cmdline/popupmenu/messages (ext_*), macOS/Windows builds.
